1→"""CLI entry point: parse args, load shared context, dispatch command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import sys
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.cli_support.parser import create_parser as _create_parser
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import LangResolutionError, resolve_lang
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import CommandRuntime
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.registry import get_command_handlers
    13→from desloppify.base.config import load_config
    14→from desloppify.base.discovery.api import set_exclusions
    15→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
    16→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    17→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_default_path, get_project_root
    18→from desloppify.base.registry import detector_names, on_detector_registered
    19→from desloppify.base.runtime_state import runtime_scope
    20→from desloppify.languages import available_langs
    21→from desloppify.state import load_state
    22→
    23→_DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    24→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _get_detector_names() -> list[str]:
    28→    """Return cached detector names, computing on first access."""
    29→    if "names" not in _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE:
    30→        _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE["names"] = detector_names()
    31→    return _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE["names"]
    32→
    33→
    34→def _invalidate_detector_names_cache() -> None:
    35→    """Invalidate detector-name cache when runtime registrations change."""
    36→    _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE.pop("names", None)
    37→
    38→
    39→on_detector_registered(_invalidate_detector_names_cache)
    40→
    41→
    42→def create_parser():
    43→    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    44→    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())
    45→
    46→
    47→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config: dict):
    48→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    49→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    50→    persisted = config.get("exclude", [])
    51→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    52→    if not combined:
    53→        return
    54→    set_exclusions(combined)
    55→    if cli_exclusions:
    56→        print(
    57→            colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    58→            file=sys.stderr,
    59→        )
    60→        return
    61→    print(
    62→        colorize(
    63→            f"  Excluding (from config): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"
    64→        ),
    65→        file=sys.stderr,
    66→    )
    67→
    68→
    69→def _resolve_default_path(args) -> None:
    70→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
    71→
    72→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
    73→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as
    74→    the preceding scan even when the project files are not under ``src/``.
    75→    """
    76→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is not None:
    77→        return
    78→    runtime_root = get_project_root()
    79→    if getattr(args, "command", None) == "review":
    80→        try:
    81→            state_file = state_path(args)
    82→            if state_file:
    83→                saved = load_state(state_file)
    84→                saved_path = saved.get("scan_path")
    85→                if saved_path:
    86→                    args.path = str((runtime_root / saved_path).resolve())
    87→                    return
    88→        except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
    89→            logger.debug("Failed to resolve default path from saved state: %s", exc)
    90→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    91→    if lang:
    92→        args.path = str(runtime_root / lang.default_src)
    93→    else:
    94→        args.path = str(get_default_path())
    95→
    96→
    97→def _load_shared_runtime(args) -> None:
    98→    """Load config/state and attach shared objects to parsed args."""
    99→    config = load_config()
   100→
   101→    state_file = state_path(args)
   102→    state = load_state(state_file)
   103→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   104→
   105→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
   106→
   107→
   108→def _resolve_handler(command: str):
   109→    return get_command_handlers()[command]
   110→
   111→
   112→def _handle_help_command(args, parser) -> None:
   113→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   114→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   115→    try:
   116→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   117→    except SystemExit:
   118→        return
   119→
   120→
   121→def main() -> None:
   122→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   123→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   124→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   125→            try:
   126→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   127→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   128→                logger.debug(
   129→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   130→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   131→                )
   132→
   133→    parser = create_parser()
   134→    args = parser.parse_args()
   135→    if args.command == "help":
   136→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   137→        return
   138→
   139→    try:
   140→        with runtime_scope():
   141→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   142→            _load_shared_runtime(args)
   143→
   144→            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
   145→            handler(args)
   146→    except CommandError as exc:
   147→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   148→        sys.exit(exc.exit_code)
   149→    except LangResolutionError as exc:
   150→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   151→        sys.exit(1)
   152→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   153→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   154→        sys.exit(1)
   155→
   156→
   157→if __name__ == "__main__":
   158→    main()
   159→

1→"""Canonical enums for issue attributes.
     2→
     3→StrEnum values compare equal to their string values (Confidence.HIGH == "high"),
     4→so existing code using raw strings continues to work during gradual migration.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import enum
    10→
    11→
    12→class Confidence(enum.StrEnum):
    13→    HIGH = "high"
    14→    MEDIUM = "medium"
    15→    LOW = "low"
    16→
    17→
    18→class Status(enum.StrEnum):
    19→    OPEN = "open"
    20→    FIXED = "fixed"
    21→    WONTFIX = "wontfix"
    22→    FALSE_POSITIVE = "false_positive"
    23→    AUTO_RESOLVED = "auto_resolved"
    24→    RESOLVED = "resolved"  # Legacy on-disk value; migrated to FIXED on load.
    25→
    26→
    27→_CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES = frozenset(
    28→    {
    29→        Status.OPEN.value,
    30→        Status.FIXED.value,
    31→        Status.WONTFIX.value,
    32→        Status.FALSE_POSITIVE.value,
    33→        Status.AUTO_RESOLVED.value,
    34→    }
    35→)
    36→_RESOLVED_STATUSES = frozenset(
    37→    {
    38→        Status.FIXED.value,
    39→        Status.WONTFIX.value,
    40→        Status.FALSE_POSITIVE.value,
    41→        Status.AUTO_RESOLVED.value,
    42→    }
    43→)
    44→_LEGACY_STATUS_ALIASES = {
    45→    Status.RESOLVED.value: Status.FIXED.value,
    46→}
    47→
    48→
    49→class Tier(enum.IntEnum):
    50→    AUTO_FIX = 1
    51→    QUICK_FIX = 2
    52→    JUDGMENT = 3
    53→    MAJOR_REFACTOR = 4
    54→
    55→
    56→def canonical_issue_status(value: object, *, default: str = Status.OPEN.value) -> str:
    57→    """Normalize legacy/unknown issue status values to a canonical token."""
    58→    token = str(value).strip().lower()
    59→    [REDACTED](token, token)
    60→    return token if token in _CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES else default
    61→
    62→
    63→def issue_status_tokens(*, include_all: bool = False) -> frozenset[str]:
    64→    """Return canonical issue-status tokens, optionally including `all`."""
    65→    if include_all:
    66→        return frozenset({*_CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES, "all"})
    67→    return _CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES
    68→
    69→
    70→def resolved_statuses() -> frozenset[str]:
    71→    """Return the set of statuses that mean an issue is no longer open."""
    72→    return _RESOLVED_STATUSES
    73→
    74→

    77→    "Status",
    78→    "Tier",
    79→    "canonical_issue_status",
    80→    "issue_status_tokens",
    81→    "resolved_statuses",
    82→]

   338→
   339→_DISPLAY_ORDER = list(DISPLAY_ORDER)
   340→
   341→JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
   342→    name for name, meta in DETECTORS.items() if meta.needs_judgment
   343→)
   344→_BASE_DETECTORS: dict[str, DetectorMeta] = dict(DETECTORS)
   345→_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER: list[str] = list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   346→_BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(JUDGMENT_DETECTORS)
   347→
   348→_on_register_callbacks: list[Callable[[], None]] = []
   349→
   350→
   351→def on_detector_registered(callback: Callable[[], None]) -> None:
   352→    """Register a callback invoked after register_detector(). No-arg."""
   353→    _on_register_callbacks.append(callback)
   354→
   355→
   356→def register_detector(meta: DetectorMeta) -> None:
   357→    """Register a detector at runtime (used by generic plugins)."""
   358→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   359→    DETECTORS[meta.name] = meta
   360→    if meta.name not in _DISPLAY_ORDER:
   361→        _DISPLAY_ORDER.append(meta.name)
   362→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = frozenset(
   363→        name for name, m in DETECTORS.items() if m.needs_judgment
   364→    )
   365→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   366→        cb()
   367→
   368→
   369→def reset_registered_detectors() -> None:
   370→    """Reset runtime-added detector registrations to built-in defaults."""
   371→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   372→    DETECTORS.clear()
   373→    DETECTORS.update(_BASE_DETECTORS)
   374→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.clear()
   375→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.extend(_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   376→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = _BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   377→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   378→        cb()
   379→
   380→
   381→def detector_names() -> list[str]:
   382→    """All registered detector names, sorted."""
   383→    return sorted(DETECTORS.keys())
   384→
   385→
   386→def display_order() -> list[str]:
   387→    """Canonical display order for terminal output."""
   388→    return list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   389→
   390→
   391→_ACTION_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   392→_ACTION_LABELS = {
   393→    "auto_fix": "autofix",
   394→    "reorganize": "move",
   395→    "refactor": "refactor",
   396→    "manual_fix": "manual",
   397→}
   398→
   399→
   400→def dimension_action_type(dim_name: str) -> str:
   401→    """Return a compact action type label for a dimension based on its detectors."""
   402→    best = "manual"
   403→    best_pri = 99
   404→    for d in DETECTORS.values():
   405→        if d.dimension == dim_name:
   406→            pri = _ACTION_PRIORITY.get(d.action_type, 99)
   407→            if pri < best_pri:
   408→                best_pri = pri
   409→                best = d.action_type
   410→    return _ACTION_LABELS.get(best, "manual")
   411→
   412→
   413→def detector_tools() -> dict[str, dict]:
   414→    """Build detector tool metadata keyed by detector name."""
   415→    result = {}
   416→    for name, d in DETECTORS.items():
   417→        entry: dict = {
   418→            "fixers": list(d.fixers),
   419→            "action_type": d.action_type,
   420→        }
   421→        if d.tool:
   422→            entry["tool"] = d.tool
   423→        if d.guidance:
   424→            entry["guidance"] = d.guidance
   425→        result[name] = entry
   426→    return result
   427→

You are an extremely systematic, detail-obsessed engineer. You see code as a formal system. You care about: precise contracts, zero ambiguity in interfaces, perfect orthogonality between components, invariants that hold everywhere, no accidental complexity, no leaky abstractions, no redundancy. You are bothered by: inconsistent return types, functions that do slightly more than their name says, parameters that are sometimes optional and sometimes not, abstraction layers that don't cleanly separate concerns, places where two things are 90% the same but diverge for no principled reason, and any code that requires you to hold more state in your head than necessary to understand it.

Your task: explore the desloppify codebase at /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify and systematically catalog what makes it merely "okay" rather than rigorous and precise. Not bugs per se — structural and design-level issues that introduce unnecessary cognitive load or accidental complexity.

Be exhaustive and specific. Quote actual code. Focus on:
- Interface contracts: are types precise? Are there implicit assumptions not captured in signatures?
- Orthogonality: do modules have clean, non-overlapping responsibilities?
- Redundancy: is the same logic expressed in multiple places?
- Coupling: what knows too much about what?
- Abstraction leaks: where does a higher layer know about lower-layer implementation details?
- Inconsistency: same concept represented differently in different places
- Hidden complexity: functions/classes that are doing more than they appear to

Key files/packages to explore: base/, engine/, engine/_plan/, intelligence/narrative/, app/commands/, languages/python/, languages/typescript/, desloppify/cli.py, desloppify/scoring.py, desloppify/state.py

1→"""CLI entry point: parse args, load shared context, dispatch command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→import sys
     7→
     8→from desloppify.app.cli_support.parser import create_parser as _create_parser
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import LangResolutionError, resolve_lang
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import CommandRuntime
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.registry import get_command_handlers
    13→from desloppify.base.config import load_config
    14→from desloppify.base.discovery.api import set_exclusions
    15→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
    16→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    17→from desloppify.base.discovery.paths import get_default_path, get_project_root
    18→from desloppify.base.registry import detector_names, on_detector_registered
    19→from desloppify.base.runtime_state import runtime_scope
    20→from desloppify.languages import available_langs
    21→from desloppify.state import load_state
    22→
    23→_DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    24→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _get_detector_names() -> list[str]:
    28→    """Return cached detector names, computing on first access."""
    29→    if "names" not in _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE:
    30→        _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE["names"] = detector_names()
    31→    return _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE["names"]
    32→
    33→
    34→def _invalidate_detector_names_cache() -> None:
    35→    """Invalidate detector-name cache when runtime registrations change."""
    36→    _DETECTOR_NAMES_CACHE.pop("names", None)
    37→
    38→
    39→on_detector_registered(_invalidate_detector_names_cache)
    40→
    41→
    42→def create_parser():
    43→    """Return the top-level argparse parser."""
    44→    return _create_parser(langs=available_langs(), detector_names=_get_detector_names())
    45→
    46→
    47→def _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config: dict):
    48→    """Merge CLI --exclude with persisted config.exclude and apply globally."""
    49→    cli_exclusions = getattr(args, "exclude", None) or []
    50→    persisted = config.get("exclude", [])
    51→    combined = list(cli_exclusions) + [e for e in persisted if e not in cli_exclusions]
    52→    if not combined:
    53→        return
    54→    set_exclusions(combined)
    55→    if cli_exclusions:
    56→        print(
    57→            colorize(f"  Excluding: {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"),
    58→            file=sys.stderr,
    59→        )
    60→        return
    61→    print(
    62→        colorize(
    63→            f"  Excluding (from config): {', '.join(combined)}", "dim"
    64→        ),
    65→        file=sys.stderr,
    66→    )
    67→
    68→
    69→def _resolve_default_path(args) -> None:
    70→    """Fill args.path from detected language or default source path.
    71→
    72→    For the review command, the last scan path (stored in state) is used as the
    73→    default so that ``desloppify review --prepare`` works on the same scope as
    74→    the preceding scan even when the project files are not under ``src/``.
    75→    """
    76→    if getattr(args, "path", None) is not None:
    77→        return
    78→    runtime_root = get_project_root()
    79→    if getattr(args, "command", None) == "review":
    80→        try:
    81→            state_file = state_path(args)
    82→            if state_file:
    83→                saved = load_state(state_file)
    84→                saved_path = saved.get("scan_path")
    85→                if saved_path:
    86→                    args.path = str((runtime_root / saved_path).resolve())
    87→                    return
    88→        except (OSError, KeyError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
    89→            logger.debug("Failed to resolve default path from saved state: %s", exc)
    90→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    91→    if lang:
    92→        args.path = str(runtime_root / lang.default_src)
    93→    else:
    94→        args.path = str(get_default_path())
    95→
    96→
    97→def _load_shared_runtime(args) -> None:
    98→    """Load config/state and attach shared objects to parsed args."""
    99→    config = load_config()
   100→
   101→    state_file = state_path(args)
   102→    state = load_state(state_file)
   103→    _apply_persisted_exclusions(args, config)
   104→
   105→    args.runtime = CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
   106→
   107→
   108→def _resolve_handler(command: str):
   109→    return get_command_handlers()[command]
   110→
   111→
   112→def _handle_help_command(args, parser) -> None:
   113→    """Handle explicit help command when present in parser config."""
   114→    topic = list(getattr(args, "topic", []) or [])
   115→    try:
   116→        parser.parse_args([*topic, "--help"])
   117→    except SystemExit:
   118→        return
   119→
   120→
   121→def main() -> None:
   122→    # Ensure Unicode output works on Windows terminals (cp1252 etc.)
   123→    for stream in (sys.stdout, sys.stderr):
   124→        if hasattr(stream, "reconfigure"):
   125→            try:
   126→                stream.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
   127→            except (AttributeError, OSError):
   128→                logger.debug(
   129→                    "Skipping stream reconfigure for %s (not supported)",
   130→                    getattr(stream, "name", "<stream>"),
   131→                )
   132→
   133→    parser = create_parser()
   134→    args = parser.parse_args()
   135→    if args.command == "help":
   136→        _handle_help_command(args, parser)
   137→        return
   138→
   139→    try:
   140→        with runtime_scope():
   141→            _resolve_default_path(args)
   142→            _load_shared_runtime(args)
   143→
   144→            handler = _resolve_handler(args.command)
   145→            handler(args)
   146→    except CommandError as exc:
   147→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   148→        sys.exit(exc.exit_code)
   149→    except LangResolutionError as exc:
   150→        print(colorize(f"  {exc.message}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
   151→        sys.exit(1)
   152→    except KeyboardInterrupt:
   153→        print("\nInterrupted.")
   154→        sys.exit(1)
   155→
   156→
   157→if __name__ == "__main__":
   158→    main()
   159→

    56→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    57→class NarrativeContext:
    58→    """Optional context inputs for narrative computation."""
    59→
    60→    diff: dict | None = None
    61→    lang: str | None = None
    62→    command: str | None = None
    63→    config: dict | None = None
    64→    plan: dict | None = None

    66→
    67→def compute_narrative(
    68→    state: StateModel,
    69→    context: NarrativeContext | None = None,
    70→) -> NarrativeResult:
    71→    """Compute structured narrative context from state data."""
    72→    resolved_context = context or NarrativeContext()
    73→
    74→    diff = resolved_context.diff
    75→    lang = resolved_context.lang
    76→    command = resolved_context.command
    77→    config = resolved_context.config
    78→    plan = resolved_context.plan
    79→
    80→    raw_history = state.get("scan_history", [])
    81→    history = _history_for_lang(raw_history, lang)
    82→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
    83→    stats = state.get("stats", {})
    84→    strict_score, overall_score = _score_snapshot(state)
    85→    issues = _scoped_issues(state)
    86→
    87→    by_detector = _count_open_by_detector(issues)
    88→    badge = _compute_badge_status()
    89→
    90→    phase = _detect_phase(history, strict_score)
    91→    dimensions = _analyze_dimensions(dim_scores, history, state)
    92→    debt = _analyze_debt(dim_scores, issues, history)
    93→    milestone = _detect_milestone(state, None, history)
    94→    clusters = plan.get("clusters") if isinstance(plan, dict) else None
    95→    action_context = ActionContext(
    96→        by_detector=by_detector,
    97→        dimension_scores=dim_scores,
    98→        state=state,
    99→        debt=debt,
   100→        lang=lang,
   101→        clusters=clusters,
   102→    )
   103→    actions = [dict(action) for action in compute_actions(action_context)]
   104→    strategy = compute_strategy(issues, by_detector, actions, phase, lang)
   105→    tools = dict(compute_tools(by_detector, state, lang, badge))
   106→    primary_action = _compute_primary_action(actions)
   107→    why_now = _compute_why_now(phase, strategy, primary_action)
   108→    verification_step = _compute_verification_step(command)
   109→    risk_flags = _compute_risk_flags(state, debt)
   110→    strict_target = _compute_strict_target(strict_score, config)
   111→    headline = _compute_headline(
   112→        phase,
   113→        dimensions,
   114→        debt,
   115→        milestone,
   116→        diff,
   117→        strict_score,
   118→        overall_score,
   119→        stats,
   120→        history,
   121→        open_by_detector=by_detector,
   122→    )
   123→    reminders, updated_reminder_history = _compute_reminders(
   124→        state, lang, phase, debt, actions, dimensions, badge, command, config=config
   125→    )
   126→
   127→    return {
   128→        "phase": phase,
   129→        "headline": headline,
   130→        "dimensions": dimensions,
   131→        "actions": actions,
   132→        "strategy": strategy,
   133→        "tools": tools,

   143→    }

1→"""Canonical enums for issue attributes.
     2→
     3→StrEnum values compare equal to their string values (Confidence.HIGH == "high"),
     4→so existing code using raw strings continues to work during gradual migration.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import enum
    10→
    11→
    12→class Confidence(enum.StrEnum):
    13→    HIGH = "high"
    14→    MEDIUM = "medium"
    15→    LOW = "low"
    16→
    17→
    18→class Status(enum.StrEnum):
    19→    OPEN = "open"
    20→    FIXED = "fixed"
    21→    WONTFIX = "wontfix"
    22→    FALSE_POSITIVE = "false_positive"
    23→    AUTO_RESOLVED = "auto_resolved"
    24→    RESOLVED = "resolved"  # Legacy on-disk value; migrated to FIXED on load.
    25→
    26→
    27→_CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES = frozenset(
    28→    {
    29→        Status.OPEN.value,
    30→        Status.FIXED.value,
    31→        Status.WONTFIX.value,
    32→        Status.FALSE_POSITIVE.value,
    33→        Status.AUTO_RESOLVED.value,
    34→    }
    35→)
    36→_RESOLVED_STATUSES = frozenset(
    37→    {
    38→        Status.FIXED.value,
    39→        Status.WONTFIX.value,
    40→        Status.FALSE_POSITIVE.value,
    41→        Status.AUTO_RESOLVED.value,
    42→    }
    43→)
    44→_LEGACY_STATUS_ALIASES = {
    45→    Status.RESOLVED.value: Status.FIXED.value,
    46→}
    47→
    48→
    49→class Tier(enum.IntEnum):
    50→    AUTO_FIX = 1
    51→    QUICK_FIX = 2
    52→    JUDGMENT = 3
    53→    MAJOR_REFACTOR = 4
    54→
    55→
    56→def canonical_issue_status(value: object, *, default: str = Status.OPEN.value) -> str:
    57→    """Normalize legacy/unknown issue status values to a canonical token."""
    58→    token = str(value).strip().lower()
    59→    [REDACTED](token, token)
    60→    return token if token in _CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES else default
    61→
    62→
    63→def issue_status_tokens(*, include_all: bool = False) -> frozenset[str]:
    64→    """Return canonical issue-status tokens, optionally including `all`."""
    65→    if include_all:
    66→        return frozenset({*_CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES, "all"})
    67→    return _CANONICAL_ISSUE_STATUSES
    68→
    69→
    70→def resolved_statuses() -> frozenset[str]:
    71→    """Return the set of statuses that mean an issue is no longer open."""
    72→    return _RESOLVED_STATUSES
    73→
    74→
    75→__all__ = [
    76→    "Confidence",
    77→    "Status",
    78→    "Tier",
    79→    "canonical_issue_status",
    80→    "issue_status_tokens",
    81→    "resolved_statuses",
    82→]

   183→def utc_now() -> str:
   184→    """Return current UTC timestamp with second-level precision."""
   185→    return datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   186→
   187→
   188→def empty_state() -> StateModel:
   189→    """Return a new empty state payload."""
   190→    return {
   191→        "version": CURRENT_VERSION,
   192→        "created": utc_now(),
   193→        "last_scan": None,
   194→        "scan_count": 0,
   195→        "overall_score": 0,
   196→        "objective_score": 0,
   197→        "strict_score": 0,
   198→        "verified_strict_score": 0,
   199→        "stats": {},
   200→        "issues": {},
   201→        "scan_coverage": {},
   202→        "score_confidence": {},
   203→        "subjective_integrity": {},
   204→        "subjective_assessments": {},
   205→    }
   206→
   207→
   208→def _as_non_negative_int(value: Any, default: int = 0) -> int:
   209→    try:
   210→        parsed = int(value)
   211→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   212→        return default
   213→    return parsed if parsed >= 0 else 0
   214→
   215→
   216→def _rename_key(d: dict, old: str, new: str) -> bool:
   217→    if old not in d:
   218→        return False
   219→    d.setdefault(new, d.pop(old))
   220→    return True
   221→
   222→
   223→def migrate_state_keys(state: dict) -> None:
   224→    """Migrate legacy key names in-place.
   225→
   226→    - ``"findings"`` → ``"issues"``
   227→    - ``dimension_scores[dim]["issues"]`` → ``"failing"``
   228→    """
   229→    _rename_key(state, "findings", "issues")
   230→
   231→    for ds in state.get("dimension_scores", {}).values():
   232→        if isinstance(ds, dict):
   233→            _rename_key(ds, "issues", "failing")
   234→
   235→    for entry in state.get("scan_history", []):
   236→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   237→            continue
   238→        _rename_key(entry, "raw_findings", "raw_issues")
   239→        for ds in (entry.get("dimension_scores") or {}).values():
   240→            if isinstance(ds, dict):
   241→                _rename_key(ds, "issues", "failing")
   242→
   243→
   244→def ensure_state_defaults(state: StateModel | dict) -> None:
   245→    """Normalize loose/legacy state payloads to a valid base shape in-place."""
   246→    migrate_state_keys(state)
   247→
   248→    for key, value in empty_state().items():
   249→        state.setdefault(key, value)
   250→
   251→    if not isinstance(state.get("issues"), dict):
   252→        state["issues"] = {}
   253→    if not isinstance(state.get("stats"), dict):
   254→        state["stats"] = {}
   255→    if not isinstance(state.get("scan_history"), list):
   256→        state["scan_history"] = []
   257→    if not isinstance(state.get("scan_coverage"), dict):
   258→        state["scan_coverage"] = {}
   259→    if not isinstance(state.get("score_confidence"), dict):
   260→        state["score_confidence"] = {}
   261→    if not isinstance(state.get("subjective_integrity"), dict):
   262→        state["subjective_integrity"] = {}
   263→
   264→    all_issues = state["issues"]
   265→    to_remove: list[str] = []
   266→    for issue_id, issue in all_issues.items():
   267→        if not isinstance(issue, dict):
   268→            to_remove.append(issue_id)
   269→            continue
   270→
   271→        issue.setdefault("id", issue_id)
   272→        issue.setdefault("detector", "unknown")
   273→        issue.setdefault("file", "")
   274→        issue.setdefault("tier", 3)
   275→        issue.setdefault("confidence", "low")
   276→        issue.setdefault("summary", "")
   277→        issue.setdefault("detail", {})
   278→        issue.setdefault("status", "open")
   279→        issue["status"] = canonical_issue_status(
   280→            issue.get("status"),
   281→            default="open",
   282→        )
   283→        issue.setdefault("note", None)
   284→        issue.setdefault("first_seen", state.get("created") or utc_now())
   285→        issue.setdefault("last_seen", issue["first_seen"])
   286→        issue.setdefault("resolved_at", None)
   287→        issue["reopen_count"] = _as_non_negative_int(
   288→            issue.get("reopen_count", 0), default=0
   289→        )
   290→        issue.setdefault("suppressed", False)
   291→        issue.setdefault("suppressed_at", None)
   292→        issue.setdefault("suppression_pattern", None)
   293→
   294→    for issue_id in to_remove:
   295→        all_issues.pop(issue_id, None)
   296→
   297→    for entry in state["scan_history"]:
   298→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   299→            continue
   300→        integrity = entry.get("subjective_integrity")
   301→        if integrity is not None and not isinstance(integrity, dict):
   302→            entry["subjective_integrity"] = None
   303→
   304→    state["scan_count"] = _as_non_negative_int(state.get("scan_count", 0), default=0)
   305→    return None
   306→
   307→
   308→def validate_state_invariants(state: StateModel) -> None:
   309→    """Raise ValueError when core state invariants are violated."""
   310→    if not isinstance(state.get("issues"), dict):
   311→        raise ValueError("state.issues must be a dict")
   312→    if not isinstance(state.get("stats"), dict):
   313→        raise ValueError("state.stats must be a dict")
   314→
   315→    all_issues = state["issues"]
   316→    for issue_id, issue in all_issues.items():
   317→        if not isinstance(issue, dict):
   318→            raise ValueError(f"issue {issue_id!r} must be a dict")
   319→        if issue.get("id") != issue_id:
   320→            raise ValueError(f"issue id mismatch for {issue_id!r}")
   321→        if issue.get("status") not in _ALLOWED_ISSUE_STATUSES:
   322→            raise ValueError(
   323→                f"issue {issue_id!r} has invalid status {issue.get('status')!r}"
   324→            )
   325→
   326→        tier = issue.get("tier")
   327→        if not isinstance(tier, int) or tier < 1 or tier > 4:
   328→            raise ValueError(f"issue {issue_id!r} has invalid tier {tier!r}")
   329→
   330→        reopen_count = issue.get("reopen_count")
   331→        if not isinstance(reopen_count, int) or reopen_count < 0:
   332→            raise ValueError(
   333→                f"issue {issue_id!r} has invalid reopen_count {reopen_count!r}"
   334→            )
   335→

    24→def coerce_assessment_score(value: object) -> float | None:
    25→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float.
    26→
    27→    Returns ``None`` when the value cannot be interpreted as a numeric score
    28→    (e.g. bools, non-numeric strings, missing keys).
    29→    """
    30→    if is_numeric(value):
    31→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value))), 1)
    32→    if isinstance(value, dict):
    33→        raw = value.get("score")
    34→        if not is_numeric(raw):
    35→            return None
    36→        return round(max(0.0, min(100.0, float(raw))), 1)
    37→    return None
    38→
    39→
    40→def _mark_stale_assessments_on_review_resolve(
    41→    state: StateModel,
    42→    *,
    43→    status: str,
    44→    resolved_issues: list[dict],
    45→    now: str,
    46→) -> None:
    47→    """Mark subjective assessments as stale when review issues are resolved.
    48→
    49→    The assessment score is preserved (not zeroed) — only a fresh review import
    50→    should change dimension scores.  The stale marker tells the UI to prompt
    51→    for a re-review.
    52→    """
    53→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    54→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    55→        return
    56→
    57→    touched_dimensions: set[str] = set()
    58→    for issue in resolved_issues:
    59→        if issue.get("detector") != "review":
    60→            continue
    61→        dimension = str(issue.get("detail", {}).get("dimension", "")).strip()
    62→        if dimension:
    63→            touched_dimensions.add(dimension)
    64→
    65→    for dimension in sorted(touched_dimensions):
    66→        if dimension not in assessments:
    67→            continue
    68→
    69→        payload = assessments.get(dimension)
    70→        if isinstance(payload, dict):
    71→            payload["needs_review_refresh"] = True
    72→            payload["refresh_reason"] = f"review_issue_{status}"
    73→            payload["stale_since"] = now
    74→        else:
    75→            assessments[dimension] = {
    76→                "score": coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0,
    77→                "needs_review_refresh": True,
    78→                "refresh_reason": f"review_issue_{status}",
    79→                "stale_since": now,
    80→            }
    81→
    82→
    83→def match_issues(
    84→    state: StateModel, pattern: str, status_filter: str = "open"
    85→) -> list[dict]:
    86→    """Return issues matching *pattern* with the given status."""
    87→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
    88→    return [
    89→        issue
    90→        for issue_id, issue in state["issues"].items()
    91→        if not issue.get("suppressed")
    92→        if (status_filter == "all" or issue["status"] == status_filter)
    93→        and _matches_pattern(issue_id, issue, pattern)
    94→    ]
    95→

    98→    state: StateModel,
    99→    pattern: str,

    34→# Mechanical detectors → subjective dimensions they provide evidence for.
    35→# When issues from these detectors change during a scan, the corresponding
    36→# subjective assessments are marked stale so reviewers know to re-evaluate.
    37→_DETECTOR_SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSIONS: dict[str, tuple[str, ...]] = {
    38→    "structural": ("design_coherence", "abstraction_fitness"),
    39→    "smells": ("design_coherence", "error_consistency"),
    40→    "global_mutable_config": ("initialization_coupling",),
    41→    "coupling": ("cross_module_architecture",),
    42→    "layer_violation": ("cross_module_architecture",),
    43→    "private_imports": ("cross_module_architecture",),
    44→    "dupes": ("convention_outlier",),
    45→    "boilerplate_duplication": ("convention_outlier",),
    46→    "naming": ("convention_outlier",),
    47→    "flat_dirs": ("package_organization",),
    48→    "orphaned": ("design_coherence",),
    49→    "uncalled_functions": ("design_coherence",),
    50→    "responsibility_cohesion": ("design_coherence", "abstraction_fitness"),
    51→    "cycles": ("cross_module_architecture", "dependency_health"),
    52→}
    53→
    54→
    55→def _mark_stale_on_mechanical_change(
    56→    state: StateModel,
    57→    *,
    58→    changed_detectors: set[str],
    59→    now: str,
    60→) -> None:
    61→    """Mark subjective assessments stale when mechanical issues change.
    62→
    63→    Only marks dimensions that already have an assessment — doesn't create
    64→    new entries for dimensions that have never been reviewed.
    65→    """
    66→    assessments = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    67→    if not isinstance(assessments, dict) or not assessments:
    68→        return
    69→
    70→    affected_dims: set[str] = set()
    71→    for detector in changed_detectors:
    72→        dims = _DETECTOR_SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSIONS.get(detector, ())
    73→        affected_dims.update(dims)
    74→
    75→    if not affected_dims:
    76→        return
    77→
    78→    for dimension in sorted(affected_dims):
    79→        if dimension not in assessments:
    80→            continue

1→"""Registry for optional language hook modules consumed by detectors."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import importlib
     6→import logging
     7→import sys
     8→from collections import defaultdict
     9→
    10→_LANG_HOOKS: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = defaultdict(dict)
    11→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    12→
    13→
    14→def register_lang_hooks(
    15→    lang_name: str,
    16→    *,
    17→    test_coverage: object | None = None,
    18→) -> None:
    19→    """Register optional detector hook modules for a language."""
    20→    hooks = _LANG_HOOKS[lang_name]
    21→    if test_coverage is not None:
    22→        hooks["test_coverage"] = test_coverage
    23→
    24→
    25→def get_lang_hook(lang_name: str | None, hook_name: str) -> object | None:
    26→    """Get a previously-registered language hook module."""
    27→    if not lang_name:
    28→        return None
    29→
    30→    hook = _LANG_HOOKS.get(lang_name, {}).get(hook_name)
    31→    if hook is not None:
    32→        return hook
    33→
    34→    module_name = f"desloppify.languages.{lang_name}"
    35→    module = sys.modules.get(module_name)
    36→
    37→    # Lazy-load only the requested language package.
    38→    if module is None:
    39→        try:
    40→            importlib.import_module(module_name)
    41→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, RuntimeError, OSError) as exc:
    42→            logger.debug(
    43→                "Unable to import language hook package %s: %s", lang_name, exc
    44→            )
    45→            return None
    46→    elif lang_name not in _LANG_HOOKS:
    47→        try:
    48→            importlib.reload(module)
    49→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, RuntimeError, OSError) as exc:
    50→            logger.debug(
    51→                "Unable to reload language hook package %s: %s", lang_name, exc
    52→            )
    53→            return None
    54→
    55→    return _LANG_HOOKS.get(lang_name, {}).get(hook_name)
    56→
    57→
    58→def clear_lang_hooks_for_tests() -> None:
    59→    """Clear registry (test helper)."""
    60→    _LANG_HOOKS.clear()
    61→
    62→
    63→__all__ = [
    64→    "clear_lang_hooks_for_tests",
    65→    "get_lang_hook",
    66→    "register_lang_hooks",
    67→]
    68→

   114→    sync_triage_needed,
   115→    sync_unscored_dimensions,
   116→)
   117→
   118→# --- subjective policy ------------------------------------------------------
   119→from desloppify.engine._plan.subjective_policy import (
   120→    compute_subjective_visibility,
   121→)
   122→
   123→
   124→def triage_phase_banner(plan: PlanModel) -> str:
   125→    """Return a banner string when triage stage IDs are in the queue."""
   126→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   127→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   128→    has_triage = any(sid in order for sid in TRIAGE_IDS)
   129→    if not has_triage:
   130→        return ""
   131→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   132→    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
   133→    completed = [s for s in ("observe", "reflect", "organize") if s in stages]
   134→    if completed:
   135→        return (
   136→            f"TRIAGE MODE ({len(completed)}/4 stages complete) — "
   137→            "complete all stages to exit. Run: desloppify plan triage"
   138→        )
   139→    return (
   140→        "TRIAGE MODE — review issues need analysis before fixing. "
   141→        "Run: desloppify plan triage"
   142→    )
   143→
   144→
   145→__all__ = [
   146→    # schema
   147→    "EPIC_PREFIX",

   339→_DISPLAY_ORDER = list(DISPLAY_ORDER)
   340→
   341→JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
   342→    name for name, meta in DETECTORS.items() if meta.needs_judgment
   343→)
   344→_BASE_DETECTORS: dict[str, DetectorMeta] = dict(DETECTORS)
   345→_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER: list[str] = list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   346→_BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(JUDGMENT_DETECTORS)
   347→
   348→_on_register_callbacks: list[Callable[[], None]] = []
   349→
   350→
   351→def on_detector_registered(callback: Callable[[], None]) -> None:
   352→    """Register a callback invoked after register_detector(). No-arg."""
   353→    _on_register_callbacks.append(callback)
   354→
   355→
   356→def register_detector(meta: DetectorMeta) -> None:
   357→    """Register a detector at runtime (used by generic plugins)."""
   358→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   359→    DETECTORS[meta.name] = meta
   360→    if meta.name not in _DISPLAY_ORDER:
   361→        _DISPLAY_ORDER.append(meta.name)
   362→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = frozenset(
   363→        name for name, m in DETECTORS.items() if m.needs_judgment
   364→    )
   365→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   366→        cb()
   367→
   368→
   369→def reset_registered_detectors() -> None:
   370→    """Reset runtime-added detector registrations to built-in defaults."""
   371→    global JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   372→    DETECTORS.clear()
   373→    DETECTORS.update(_BASE_DETECTORS)
   374→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.clear()
   375→    _DISPLAY_ORDER.extend(_BASE_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   376→    JUDGMENT_DETECTORS = _BASE_JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   377→    for cb in _on_register_callbacks:
   378→        cb()
   379→
   380→
   381→def detector_names() -> list[str]:
   382→    """All registered detector names, sorted."""
   383→    return sorted(DETECTORS.keys())
   384→
   385→
   386→def display_order() -> list[str]:
   387→    """Canonical display order for terminal output."""
   388→    return list(_DISPLAY_ORDER)
   389→
   390→
   391→_ACTION_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "reorganize": 1, "refactor": 2, "manual_fix": 3}
   392→_ACTION_LABELS = {
   393→    "auto_fix": "autofix",
   394→    "reorganize": "move",
   395→    "refactor": "refactor",
   396→    "manual_fix": "manual",
   397→}
   398→
   399→
   400→def dimension_action_type(dim_name: str) -> str:
   401→    """Return a compact action type label for a dimension based on its detectors."""
   402→    best = "manual"
   403→    best_pri = 99
   404→    for d in DETECTORS.values():
   405→        if d.dimension == dim_name:
   406→            pri = _ACTION_PRIORITY.get(d.action_type, 99)
   407→            if pri < best_pri:
   408→                best_pri = pri
   409→                best = d.action_type
   410→    return _ACTION_LABELS.get(best, "manual")
   411→
   412→
   413→def detector_tools() -> dict[str, dict]:
   414→    """Build detector tool metadata keyed by detector name."""
   415→    result = {}
   416→    for name, d in DETECTORS.items():
   417→        entry: dict = {
   418→            "fixers": list(d.fixers),
   419→            "action_type": d.action_type,
   420→        }
   421→        if d.tool:
   422→            entry["tool"] = d.tool
   423→        if d.guidance:
   424→            entry["guidance"] = d.guidance
   425→        result[name] = entry
   426→    return result
   427→

1→"""Language registry: plugin registration and language resolution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import inspect
     6→from collections.abc import Callable
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→from typing import TypeVar
     9→
    10→from desloppify.languages._framework import (
    11→    discovery,
    12→    registry_state,
    13→    resolution,
    14→    runtime,
    15→)
    16→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import LangConfig
    17→from desloppify.languages._framework.contract_validation import validate_lang_contract
    18→from desloppify.languages._framework.policy import REQUIRED_DIRS, REQUIRED_FILES
    19→from desloppify.languages._framework.resolution import (
    20→    auto_detect_lang,
    21→    available_langs,
    22→    get_lang,
    23→    make_lang_config,
    24→)
    25→from desloppify.languages._framework.structure_validation import validate_lang_structure
    26→
    27→T = TypeVar("T")
    28→
    29→
    30→def register_lang(name: str) -> Callable[[T], T]:
    31→    """Decorator to register a language config class.
    32→
    33→    Validates structure, instantiates the class, validates the contract,
    34→    and stores the *instance* in the registry.
    35→    """
    36→
    37→    def decorator(cls: T) -> T:
    38→        module = inspect.getmodule(cls)
    39→        if module and hasattr(module, "__file__"):
    40→            validate_lang_structure(Path(module.__file__).parent, name)
    41→        if isinstance(cls, type) and issubclass(cls, LangConfig):
    42→            cfg = make_lang_config(name, cls)  # instantiate + validate
    43→            registry_state.register(name, cfg)  # store instance
    44→        else:
    45→            registry_state.register(name, cls)  # test doubles
    46→        return cls
    47→
    48→    return decorator
    49→
    50→
    51→def register_generic_lang(name: str, cfg: LangConfig) -> None:
    52→    """Register a pre-built language plugin instance (no package structure required)."""
    53→    validate_lang_contract(name, cfg)
    54→    registry_state.register(name, cfg)
    55→
    56→
    57→def reload_lang_plugins() -> list[str]:
    58→    """Force plugin rediscovery and return refreshed language names."""
    59→    discovery.load_all(force_reload=True)
    60→    return sorted(registry_state.all_keys())
    61→
    62→
    63→__all__ = [
    64→    "REQUIRED_FILES",
    65→    "REQUIRED_DIRS",
    66→    "register_lang",
    67→    "register_generic_lang",
    68→    "reload_lang_plugins",
    69→    "get_lang",
    70→    "available_langs",
    71→    "auto_detect_lang",
    72→    "make_lang_config",
    73→    "validate_lang_structure",
    74→    "validate_lang_contract",
    75→    "discovery",
    76→    "registry_state",
    77→    "resolution",
    78→    "runtime",
    79→]
    80→

     7→
     8→
     9→@dataclass
    10→class FunctionInfo:
    11→    """Extracted function/method info for cross-language analysis."""
    12→
    13→    name: str
    14→    file: str
    15→    line: int
    16→    end_line: int
    17→    loc: int
    18→    body: str
    19→    normalized: str = ""
    20→    body_hash: str = ""
    21→    params: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    22→    return_annotation: str | None = None
    23→
    24→
    25→@dataclass
    26→class ClassInfo:
    27→    """Extracted class/component info for cross-language analysis.
    28→
    29→    For OOP classes: methods/attributes/base_classes are populated directly.
    30→    For React components: metrics holds hook counts (context_hooks, use_effects, etc.).
    31→    """
    32→
    33→    name: str
    34→    file: str
    35→    line: int
    36→    loc: int
    37→    methods: list[FunctionInfo] = field(default_factory=list)
    38→    attributes: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    39→    base_classes: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    40→    metrics: dict[str, int] = field(default_factory=dict)
    41→
    42→
    43→@dataclass
    44→class ComplexitySignal:
    45→    """A complexity signal to detect in source files.
    46→
    47→    Either pattern-based (regex matched per line, counted) or compute-based
    48→    (custom function that analyzes content and returns count + label).
    49→    """
    50→
    51→    name: str
    52→    pattern: str | None = None  # regex (None = uses compute fn)
    53→    weight: int = 1
    54→    threshold: int = 0
    55→    compute: Callable | None = None  # (content, lines) -> (count, label) | None
    56→
    57→
    58→@dataclass
    59→class GodRule:
    60→    """A rule for detecting god classes/components.
    61→
    62→    The extract callable pulls a metric from ClassInfo; if it meets the threshold,
    63→    the rule fires and contributes to the "reasons" list.
    64→    """
    65→
    66→    name: str
    67→    description: str
    68→    extract: Callable  # (ClassInfo) -> int
    69→    threshold: int
    70→
    71→

1→"""Score/value accessor helpers for state schema payloads."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any
     7→
     8→if TYPE_CHECKING:
     9→    from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    10→
    11→
    12→def json_default(obj: Any) -> Any:
    13→    """JSON serializer fallback for known non-JSON-native state values."""
    14→    if isinstance(obj, set):
    15→        return sorted(obj)
    16→    if isinstance(obj, Path):
    17→        return str(obj).replace("\\", "/")
    18→    if hasattr(obj, "isoformat"):
    19→        return obj.isoformat()
    20→    raise TypeError(
    21→        f"Object of type {type(obj).__name__} is not JSON serializable: {obj!r}"
    22→    )
    23→
    24→
    25→def _get_overall_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
    26→    value = state.get("overall_score")
    27→    return float(value) if isinstance(value, int | float) else None
    28→
    29→
    30→def _get_objective_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
    31→    value = state.get("objective_score")
    32→    return float(value) if isinstance(value, int | float) else None
    33→
    34→
    35→def _get_strict_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
    36→    value = state.get("strict_score")
    37→    return float(value) if isinstance(value, int | float) else None
    38→
    39→
    40→def _get_verified_strict_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
    41→    value = state.get("verified_strict_score")
    42→    return float(value) if isinstance(value, int | float) else None
    43→
    44→

    47→    "_get_overall_score",
    48→    "_get_strict_score",

    67→def compute_narrative(
    68→    state: StateModel,
    69→    context: NarrativeContext | None = None,
    70→) -> NarrativeResult:

    74→    diff = resolved_context.diff

    76→    command = resolved_context.command
    77→    config = resolved_context.config
    78→    plan = resolved_context.plan

    82→    dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})

    85→    issues = _scoped_issues(state)
    86→
    87→    by_detector = _count_open_by_detector(issues)
    88→    badge = _compute_badge_status()
    89→
    90→    phase = _detect_phase(history, strict_score)
    91→    dimensions = _analyze_dimensions(dim_scores, history, state)
    92→    debt = _analyze_debt(dim_scores, issues, history)
    93→    milestone = _detect_milestone(state, None, history)
    94→    clusters = plan.get("clusters") if isinstance(plan, dict) else None
    95→    action_context = ActionContext(
    96→        by_detector=by_detector,
    97→        dimension_scores=dim_scores,

     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6→from typing import Any, NotRequired, Required, TypedDict
     7→
     8→REVIEW_ISSUE_REQUIRED_FIELDS = (
     9→    "dimension",
    10→    "identifier",
    11→    "summary",
    12→    "confidence",
    13→    "suggestion",
    14→    "related_files",
    15→    "evidence",

    17→VALID_REVIEW_CONFIDENCE = frozenset({"high", "medium", "low"})
    18→
    19→
    20→class ReviewIssuePayload(TypedDict, total=False):
    21→    """Single issue entry in review import payloads."""
    22→
    23→    file: str
    24→    dimension: str
    25→    identifier: str
    26→    summary: str
    27→    confidence: str
    28→    suggestion: str
    29→    evidence: list[str]
    30→    related_files: list[str]
    31→    reasoning: str
    32→    evidence_lines: list[int]
    33→    concern_verdict: str
    34→    concern_fingerprint: str
    35→    concern_type: str
    36→    concern_file: str
    37→
    38→
    39→def _normalized_non_empty_text(value: object) -> str | None:
    40→    if not isinstance(value, str):
    41→        return None
    42→    text = value.strip()
    43→    return text if text else None
    44→
    45→
    46→def _normalized_non_empty_text_list(value: object) -> list[str] | None:
    47→    if not isinstance(value, list):
    48→        return None
    49→    cleaned = [
    50→        str(item).strip()
    51→        for item in value
    52→        if isinstance(item, str) and item.strip()
    53→    ]
    54→    return cleaned if cleaned else None
    55→
    56→
    57→def validate_review_issue_payload(
    58→    issue: object,
    59→    *,
    60→    label: str,
    61→    allowed_dimensions: set[str] | None = None,
    62→    allow_dismissed: bool = True,
    63→) -> tuple[ReviewIssuePayload | None, list[str]]:
    64→    """Validate and normalize one review issue payload entry."""
    65→    if not isinstance(issue, dict):
    66→        return None, [f"{label} must be an object"]
    67→
    68→    dismissed = issue.get("concern_verdict") == "dismissed"
    69→    if dismissed and not allow_dismissed:
    70→        return None, [f"{label}.concern_verdict='dismissed' is not allowed here"]
    71→
    72→    if dismissed:
    73→        fingerprint = _normalized_non_empty_text(issue.get("concern_fingerprint"))
    74→        if fingerprint is None:
    75→            return (
    76→                None,
    77→                [
    78→                    f"{label}.concern_fingerprint is required when concern_verdict='dismissed'"
    79→                ],
    80→            )
    81→        normalized: ReviewIssuePayload = {
    82→            "concern_verdict": "dismissed",
    83→            "concern_fingerprint": fingerprint,
    84→        }
    85→        concern_type = _normalized_non_empty_text(issue.get("concern_type"))
    86→        if concern_type is not None:
    87→            normalized["concern_type"] = concern_type
    88→        concern_file = _normalized_non_empty_text(issue.get("concern_file"))
    89→        if concern_file is not None:
    90→            normalized["concern_file"] = concern_file
    91→        reasoning = _normalized_non_empty_text(issue.get("reasoning"))
    92→        if reasoning is not None:
    93→            normalized["reasoning"] = reasoning
    94→        return normalized, []
    95→
    96→    errors: list[str] = []
    97→    missing = [field for field in REVIEW_ISSUE_REQUIRED_FIELDS if field not in issue]
    98→    if missing:
    99→        errors.append(f"{label} missing required fields: {', '.join(missing)}")
   100→        return None, errors

    38→from desloppify.state import score_snapshot
    39→
    40→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    41→
    42→
    43→def generate_scorecard(state: dict, output_path: str | Path) -> Path:
    44→    """Render a landscape scorecard PNG from scan state. Returns the output path."""
    45→    image_mod = importlib.import_module("PIL.Image")  # deferred: optional dependency
    46→    image_draw_mod = importlib.import_module("PIL.ImageDraw")  # deferred: optional dependency
    47→    scorecard_draw_mod = importlib.import_module("desloppify.app.output.scorecard_parts.draw")  # deferred: depends on PIL
    48→
    49→    output_path = Path(output_path)
    50→
    51→    scores = score_snapshot(state)
    52→    main_score = scores.overall or 0
    53→    strict_score = scores.strict or 0
    54→
    55→    project_name = resolve_project_name(get_project_root())
    56→    package_version = resolve_package_version(
    57→        get_project_root(),
    58→        version_getter=importlib_metadata.version,
    59→        package_not_found_error=importlib_metadata.PackageNotFoundError,
    60→    )
    61→
    62→    # Layout — landscape (wide), File health first
    63→    active_dims = prepare_scorecard_dimensions(state)
    64→    row_count = len(active_dims)
    65→    row_h = scale(20)
    66→    width = scale(780)
    67→    divider_x = scale(260)
    68→    frame_inset = scale(5)
    69→
    70→    cols = 2
    71→    rows_per_col = (row_count + cols - 1) // cols
    72→    table_content_h = scale(14) + scale(4) + scale(6) + rows_per_col * row_h
    73→    content_h = max(table_content_h + scale(28), scale(150))
    74→    height = scale(12) + content_h
    75→
    76→    img = image_mod.new("RGB", (width, height), BG)
    77→    draw = image_draw_mod.Draw(img)
    78→
    79→    # Double frame
    80→    draw.rectangle((0, 0, width - 1, height - 1), outline=FRAME, width=scale(2))

1→"""Path resolution and exclusion matching helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→import os
     7→import tempfile
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.base.text.text_api import get_project_root
    11→
    12→
    13→def matches_exclusion(rel_path: str, exclusion: str) -> bool:
    14→    """Check if a relative path matches an exclusion pattern."""
    15→    parts = Path(rel_path).parts
    16→    if exclusion in parts:
    17→        return True
    18→    if "*" in exclusion:
    19→        if any(fnmatch.fnmatch(part, exclusion) for part in parts):
    20→            return True
    21→        # Full-path glob match for patterns with directory separators
    22→        # (e.g. "Wan2GP/**" should match "Wan2GP/models/rf.py").
    23→        if "/" in exclusion or os.sep in exclusion:
    24→            normalized_path = rel_path.lstrip("./")
    25→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(normalized_path, exclusion):
    26→                return True
    27→    if "/" in exclusion or os.sep in exclusion:
    28→        normalized = exclusion.rstrip("/").rstrip(os.sep)
    29→        return rel_path == normalized or rel_path.startswith(normalized + "/") or rel_path.startswith(
    30→            normalized + os.sep
    31→        )
    32→    return False
    33→
    34→
    35→def normalize_path_separators(path: str) -> str:
    36→    return path.replace("\\", "/")
    37→
    38→
    39→def safe_relpath(path: str | Path, start: str | Path) -> str:
    40→    try:
    41→        return os.path.relpath(str(path), str(start))
    42→    except ValueError:
    43→        return str(Path(path).resolve())
    44→
    45→
    46→def rel(path: str) -> str:
    47→    root = get_project_root()
    48→    resolved = Path(path).resolve()
    49→    try:
    50→        return normalize_path_separators(str(resolved.relative_to(root)))
    51→    except ValueError:
    52→        return normalize_path_separators(safe_relpath(resolved, root))
    53→
    54→
    55→def resolve_path(filepath: str) -> str:
    56→    """Resolve a filepath to absolute, handling both relative and absolute."""
    57→    p = Path(filepath)
    58→    if p.is_absolute():
    59→        return str(p.resolve())
    60→    return str((get_project_root() / filepath).resolve())
    61→
    62→
    63→def resolve_scan_file(
    64→    filepath: str | Path,
    65→    *,
    66→    scan_root: str | Path | None = None,
    67→) -> Path:
    68→    """Resolve a scan file path with explicit scan-root-first semantics.
    69→
    70→    Relative file paths are resolved against ``scan_root`` first (when provided)
    71→    and then against the process project root as a fallback.
    72→    """
    73→    p = Path(filepath)
    74→    if p.is_absolute():
    75→        return p.resolve()
    76→
    77→    root = get_project_root()
    78→    if scan_root is not None:
    79→        scan_root_path = Path(scan_root)
    80→        scan_root_abs = (
    81→            scan_root_path.resolve()
    82→            if scan_root_path.is_absolute()
    83→            else (root / scan_root_path).resolve()
    84→        )
    85→        scan_candidate = (scan_root_abs / p).resolve()
    86→        if scan_candidate.exists():
    87→            return scan_candidate
    88→
    89→    return (root / p).resolve()
    90→
    91→
    92→def safe_write_text(filepath: str | Path, content: str) -> None:
    93→    """Atomically write text to a file using temp+rename."""
    94→    p = Path(filepath)
    95→    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    96→    fd, tmp = tempfile.mkstemp(dir=p.parent, suffix=".tmp")
    97→    try:
    98→        with os.fdopen(fd, "w") as f:
    99→            f.write(content)
   100→        os.replace(tmp, str(p))

1→"""Shared exception tuples used across command/render flows."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→
     6→class CommandError(Exception):
     7→    """Raised by command helpers to signal a user-facing CLI error.
     8→
     9→    The CLI entrypoint catches this and prints ``message`` to stderr
    10→    before exiting with ``exit_code``.  Helpers should raise this instead
    11→    of calling ``sys.exit()`` directly so that the error path is testable
    12→    and composable.
    13→    """
    14→
    15→    def __init__(self, message: str, *, exit_code: int = 1) -> None:
    16→        self.message = message
    17→        self.exit_code = exit_code
    18→        super().__init__(message)
    19→
    20→
    21→class PacketValidationError(CommandError):
    22→    """Raised for malformed/missing review packet inputs."""
    23→
    24→
    25→class RunnerTimeoutError(CommandError):
    26→    """Raised when review batch execution fails due to timeout conditions."""
    27→
    28→
    29→class TriageValidationError(CommandError):
    30→    """Raised for invalid triage-stage attestation or workflow inputs."""

1→"""State-path and scan-gating helpers for command modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import auto_detect_lang_name
     8→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
     9→from desloppify.base.text.text_api import get_project_root
    10→
    11→
    12→def _sole_existing_lang_state_file() -> Path | None:
    13→    """Return the only existing language-specific state file, if unambiguous."""
    14→    state_dir = get_project_root() / ".desloppify"
    15→    if not state_dir.exists():
    16→        return None
    17→    candidates = sorted(path for path in state_dir.glob("state-*.json") if path.is_file())
    18→    if len(candidates) == 1:
    19→        return candidates[0]
    20→    return None
    21→
    22→
    23→def _allow_lang_state_fallback(args) -> bool:
    24→    """Whether command can safely fallback to the sole existing lang state file."""
    25→    # Scan should always honor detected/explicit language mapping to avoid cross-lang merges.
    26→    return getattr(args, "command", None) != "scan"
    27→
    28→
    29→def state_path(args) -> Path | None:
    30→    """Get state file path from args, or None for default."""
    31→    path_arg = getattr(args, "state", None)
    32→    if path_arg:
    33→        return Path(path_arg)
    34→    lang_name = getattr(args, "lang", None)
    35→    if not lang_name:
    36→        lang_name = auto_detect_lang_name(args)
    37→    if lang_name:
    38→        resolved = get_project_root() / ".desloppify" / f"state-{lang_name}.json"
    39→        if resolved.exists() or not _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    40→            return resolved
    41→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    42→        if fallback is not None:
    43→            return fallback
    44→        return resolved
    45→
    46→    if _allow_lang_state_fallback(args):
    47→        fallback = _sole_existing_lang_state_file()
    48→        if fallback is not None:
    49→            return fallback
    50→    return None
    51→
    52→
    53→def require_completed_scan(state: dict) -> bool:
    54→    """Return True when the state contains at least one completed scan."""
    55→    has_completed_scan = bool(state.get("last_scan"))
    56→    if not has_completed_scan:
    57→        print(colorize("No scans yet. Run: desloppify scan", "yellow"))
    58→    return has_completed_scan

     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    11→from desloppify.base.config import load_config
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    13→
    14→
    15→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    16→class CommandRuntime:
    17→    """Explicit runtime dependencies shared by command handlers."""
    18→
    19→    config: dict[str, Any]
    20→    state: StateModel
    21→    state_path: Path | None
    22→
    23→
    24→def command_runtime(args) -> CommandRuntime:
    25→    """Return runtime context from explicit args.runtime or construct one."""
    26→    runtime = getattr(args, "runtime", None)
    27→    if isinstance(runtime, CommandRuntime):
    28→        return runtime
    29→
    30→    config = load_config()
    31→    state_file = state_path(args)
    32→    if isinstance(state_file, str):
    33→        state_file = Path(state_file)
    34→
    35→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    36→
    37→    return CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
    38→
    39→
    40→__all__ = ["CommandRuntime", "command_runtime"]

1→"""State scoring, statistics, and suppression accounting."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from copy import deepcopy
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "suppression_metrics",
     9→]
    10→
    11→from desloppify.base.coercions import coerce_confidence
    12→from desloppify.base.enums import issue_status_tokens
    13→from desloppify.engine._scoring.policy.core import matches_target_score
    14→from desloppify.engine._state.filtering import path_scoped_issues
    15→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, ensure_state_defaults
    16→from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import ScanCoverageRecord
    17→
    18→_EMPTY_COUNTERS = tuple(sorted(issue_status_tokens()))
    19→_SUBJECTIVE_TARGET_RESET_THRESHOLD = 2
    20→
    21→
    22→def _resolve_lang_from_state(state: StateModel) -> str | None:
    23→    """Best-effort language detection from state (scan_history > lang_capabilities)."""
    24→    history = state.get("scan_history")
    25→    if isinstance(history, list):
    26→        for entry in reversed(history):
    27→            if isinstance(entry, dict):
    28→                lang = entry.get("lang")
    29→                if isinstance(lang, str) and lang.strip():
    30→                    return lang.strip().lower()
    31→    capabilities = state.get("lang_capabilities")
    32→    if isinstance(capabilities, dict) and len(capabilities) == 1:
    33→        only_lang = next(iter(capabilities.keys()))
    34→        if isinstance(only_lang, str) and only_lang.strip():
    35→            return only_lang.strip().lower()
    36→    return None
    37→
    38→
    39→def _count_issues(issues: dict) -> tuple[dict[str, int], dict[int, dict[str, int]]]:
    40→    """Tally per-status counters and per-tier breakdowns."""
    41→    counters = dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0)
    42→    tier_stats: dict[int, dict[str, int]] = {}
    43→
    44→    for issue in issues.values():
    45→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
    46→            continue
    47→        status = issue["status"]
    48→        tier = issue.get("tier", 3)
    49→        counters[status] = counters.get(status, 0) + 1
    50→        tier_counter = tier_stats.setdefault(tier, dict.fromkeys(_EMPTY_COUNTERS, 0))
    51→        tier_counter[status] = tier_counter.get(status, 0) + 1
    52→
    53→    return counters, tier_stats
    54→
    55→
    56→def _coerce_subjective_score(value: dict | float | int | str | None) -> float:
    57→    """Normalize a subjective assessment score payload to a 0-100 float."""
    58→    raw = value.get("score", 0) if isinstance(value, dict) else value
    59→    try:
    60→        score = float(raw)
    61→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    62→        score = 0.0
    63→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, score))
    64→
    65→
    66→def _subjective_target_matches(
    67→    subjective_assessments: dict, *, target: float
    68→) -> list[str]:
    69→    """Return dimension keys whose subjective score matches the target band."""
    70→    matches = [
    71→        dimension
    72→        for dimension, payload in subjective_assessments.items()
    73→        if matches_target_score(_coerce_subjective_score(payload), target)
    74→    ]
    75→    return sorted(matches)
    76→
    77→
    78→def _subjective_integrity_baseline(target: float | None) -> dict[str, object]:
    79→    """Create baseline subjective-integrity metadata for scan/reporting output."""
    80→    return {
    81→        "status": "disabled" if target is None else "pass",
    82→        "target_score": None if target is None else round(float(target), 2),
    83→        "matched_count": 0,
    84→        "matched_dimensions": [],
    85→        "reset_dimensions": [],
    86→    }
    87→
    88→
    89→def _apply_subjective_integrity_policy(
    90→    subjective_assessments: dict,
    91→    *,
    92→    target: float,
    93→) -> tuple[dict, dict[str, object]]:
    94→    """Apply anti-gaming penalties for subjective scores clustered on the target."""
    95→    normalized_target = max(0.0, min(100.0, float(target)))
    96→    matched_dimensions = _subjective_target_matches(
    97→        subjective_assessments,
    98→        target=normalized_target,
    99→    )
   100→    meta = _subjective_integrity_baseline(normalized_target)

   104→def _merge_prompt_display_and_weights(
   105→    payload: dict[str, object],
   106→    *,
   107→    prompt_meta: dict[str, object],
   108→    override_existing: bool,
   109→) -> None:
   110→    if "display_name" in prompt_meta and (
   111→        override_existing or "display_name" not in payload
   112→    ):
   113→        payload["display_name"] = prompt_meta["display_name"]
   114→    if "weight" in prompt_meta and (override_existing or "weight" not in payload):
   115→        payload["weight"] = prompt_meta["weight"]
   116→    if "reset_on_scan" in prompt_meta and (
   117→        override_existing or "reset_on_scan" not in payload
   118→    ):
   119→        payload["reset_on_scan"] = prompt_meta["reset_on_scan"]
   120→
   121→
   122→def _merge_enabled_by_default_flag(
   123→    payload: dict[str, object],
   124→    *,
   125→    prompt_meta: dict[str, object],
   126→    override_existing: bool,
   127→    default_enabled: bool,
   128→) -> None:
   129→    if default_enabled:
   130→        payload["enabled_by_default"] = True
   131→    if "enabled_by_default" not in prompt_meta:
   132→        return
   133→    prompt_enabled = bool(prompt_meta["enabled_by_default"])
   134→    if override_existing:
   135→        payload["enabled_by_default"] = prompt_enabled
   136→        return
   137→    payload["enabled_by_default"] = bool(
   138→        payload.get("enabled_by_default", False) or prompt_enabled
   139→    )
   140→
   141→
   142→def _normalized_default_dimensions(dimensions: list[str]) -> set[str]:
   143→    return {
   144→        _normalize_dimension_name(dim)
   145→        for dim in dimensions
   146→        if isinstance(dim, str) and dim.strip()
   147→    }
   148→
   149→
   150→def _merge_dimension_meta(
   151→    target: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
   152→    *,
   153→    dimensions: list[str],
   154→    prompts: dict[str, dict[str, object]],
   155→    override_existing: bool = False,
   156→) -> None:
   157→    defaults = _normalized_default_dimensions(dimensions)
   158→
   159→    for raw_dim, entry in prompts.items():
   160→        dim = _normalize_dimension_name(raw_dim)
   161→        if not dim:
   162→            continue
   163→
   164→        payload = target.setdefault(dim, {})
   165→        prompt_meta = extract_prompt_meta(entry)
   166→        _merge_prompt_display_and_weights(
   167→            payload,
   168→            prompt_meta=prompt_meta,
   169→            override_existing=override_existing,
   170→        )
   171→        _merge_enabled_by_default_flag(
   172→            payload,
   173→            prompt_meta=prompt_meta,
   174→            override_existing=override_existing,
   175→            default_enabled=dim in defaults,
   176→        )
   177→
   178→
   179→def _default_available_languages() -> list[str]:

   198→class _SubjectiveProviderState:

   202→        )
   203→        self.load_dimensions_payload_provider: Callable[
   204→            [], tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]

   206→        self.load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider: Callable[

   208→        ] = _default_load_dimensions_payload_for_lang
   209→
   210→
   211→_PROVIDER_STATE = _SubjectiveProviderState()

   220→
   221→
   222→def _load_dimensions_payload_for_lang(
   223→    lang_name: str,
