   226→
   227→
   228→def _clear_subjective_dimension_caches() -> None:
   229→    default_dimension_keys.cache_clear()
   230→    default_dimension_keys_for_lang.cache_clear()
   231→    load_subjective_dimension_metadata.cache_clear()
   232→    load_subjective_dimension_metadata_for_lang.cache_clear()
   233→
   234→
   235→def configure_subjective_dimension_providers(
   236→    *,
   237→    available_languages_provider: Callable[[], list[str]] | None = None,
   238→    load_dimensions_payload_provider: Callable[
   239→        [], tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]
   240→    ]
   241→    | None = None,
   242→    load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider: Callable[
   243→        [str], tuple[list[str], dict[str, dict[str, object]], str]
   244→    ]
   245→    | None = None,
   246→) -> None:
   247→    """Configure metadata providers for subjective-dimension lookups."""
   248→    state = _PROVIDER_STATE
   249→
   250→    changed = False
   251→    if (
   252→        available_languages_provider is not None
   253→        and available_languages_provider is not state.available_languages_provider
   254→    ):
   255→        state.available_languages_provider = available_languages_provider
   256→        changed = True
   257→    if (
   258→        load_dimensions_payload_provider is not None
   259→        and load_dimensions_payload_provider
   260→        is not state.load_dimensions_payload_provider
   261→    ):
   262→        state.load_dimensions_payload_provider = load_dimensions_payload_provider
   263→        changed = True
   264→    if (
   265→        load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider is not None
   266→        and load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider
   267→        is not state.load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider
   268→    ):
   269→        state.load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider = (
   270→            load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider
   271→        )
   272→        changed = True
   273→
   274→    if changed:
   275→        _clear_subjective_dimension_caches()
   276→
   277→
   278→def reset_subjective_dimension_providers() -> None:
   279→    """Reset metadata providers to built-in defaults."""
   280→    configure_subjective_dimension_providers(
   281→        available_languages_provider=_default_available_languages,
   282→        load_dimensions_payload_provider=_default_load_dimensions_payload,
   283→        load_dimensions_payload_for_lang_provider=_default_load_dimensions_payload_for_lang,
   284→    )
   285→
   286→
   287→def _normalize_dimension_list(values: list[str]) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   288→    normalized: list[str] = []
   289→    for raw in values:
   290→        if not isinstance(raw, str):
   291→            continue
   292→        dim = _normalize_dimension_name(raw)
   293→        if not dim or dim in normalized:
   294→            continue
   295→        normalized.append(dim)
   296→    return tuple(normalized)
   297→
   298→
   299→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
   300→def default_dimension_keys() -> tuple[str, ...]:
   301→    """Return canonical default subjective dimension keys."""
   302→    try:
   303→        dims, _, _ = _load_dimensions_payload()
   304→    except (ImportError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as exc:
   305→        logger.debug("Failed to load default subjective dimensions: %s", exc)
   306→        return tuple(_LEGACY_DISPLAY_NAMES.keys())
   307→    return _normalize_dimension_list(dims)
   308→
   309→
   310→@lru_cache(maxsize=16)
   311→def default_dimension_keys_for_lang(lang_name: str | None) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   312→    """Return default subjective dimension keys for a specific language."""
   313→    normalized = _normalize_lang_name(lang_name)
   314→    if normalized is None:
   315→        return default_dimension_keys()
   316→    try:
   317→        dims, _, _ = _load_dimensions_payload_for_lang(normalized)
   318→    except (ImportError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as exc:
   319→        logger.debug(
   320→            "Failed to load subjective dimensions for lang %s: %s",
   321→            normalized,
   322→            exc,
   323→        )
   324→        return default_dimension_keys()
   325→    return _normalize_dimension_list(dims)
   326→
   327→
   328→def _build_subjective_dimension_metadata(
   329→    *,
   330→    lang_name: str | None,
   331→) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
   332→    out: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = {}
   333→
   334→    try:
   335→        shared_defaults, shared_prompts, _ = _load_dimensions_payload()
   336→    except (ImportError, ValueError, RuntimeError) as exc:
   337→        logger.debug("Failed to load shared subjective dimension payload: %s", exc)
   338→        shared_defaults, shared_prompts = [], {}
   339→    _merge_dimension_meta(out, dimensions=shared_defaults, prompts=shared_prompts)
   340→
   341→    langs = (
   342→        [lang_name]
   343→        if isinstance(lang_name, str) and lang_name.strip()
   344→        else _available_languages()
   345→    )
   346→    for name in langs:
   347→        try:
   348→            lang_defaults, lang_prompts, _ = _load_dimensions_payload_for_lang(name)
   349→            _merge_dimension_meta(
   350→                out,
   351→                dimensions=lang_defaults,
   352→                prompts=lang_prompts,
   353→                override_existing=bool(lang_name),
   354→            )
   355→        except (ValueError, RuntimeError) as exc:
   356→            logger.debug("Failed to load dimensions for lang %s: %s", name, exc)
   357→            continue
   358→
   359→    for dim, payload in out.items():
   360→        payload.setdefault(
   361→            "display_name",
   362→            _LEGACY_DISPLAY_NAMES.get(dim, _title_display_name(dim)),
   363→        )
   364→        payload.setdefault("weight", _LEGACY_WEIGHT_BY_DIMENSION.get(dim, 1.0))
   365→        payload.setdefault("enabled_by_default", False)
   366→        if dim in _LEGACY_DISPLAY_NAMES:
   367→            payload.setdefault("reset_on_scan", dim in _LEGACY_RESET_ON_SCAN_DIMENSIONS)
   368→        else:
   369→            payload.setdefault("reset_on_scan", True)
   370→
   371→    # Preserve legacy dimensions even if a payload temporarily drops one.
   372→    for dim, display in _LEGACY_DISPLAY_NAMES.items():
   373→        payload = out.setdefault(dim, {})
   374→        payload.setdefault("display_name", display)
   375→        payload.setdefault("weight", _LEGACY_WEIGHT_BY_DIMENSION.get(dim, 1.0))
   376→        payload.setdefault("enabled_by_default", True)
   377→        payload.setdefault("reset_on_scan", dim in _LEGACY_RESET_ON_SCAN_DIMENSIONS)
   378→
   379→    return out
   380→
   381→
   382→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
   383→def load_subjective_dimension_metadata() -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
   384→    """Return merged metadata across all known dimensions/languages."""
   385→    return _build_subjective_dimension_metadata(lang_name=None)
   386→
   387→
   388→@lru_cache(maxsize=16)
   389→def load_subjective_dimension_metadata_for_lang(
   390→    lang_name: str | None,
   391→) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
   392→    """Return merged metadata for one language (with language overrides)."""
   393→    normalized = _normalize_lang_name(lang_name)
   394→    return _build_subjective_dimension_metadata(lang_name=normalized)
   395→
   396→
   397→def _metadata_registry(lang_name: str | None) -> dict[str, dict[str, object]]:
   398→    normalized = _normalize_lang_name(lang_name)
   399→    if normalized is None:
   400→        return load_subjective_dimension_metadata()
   401→    return load_subjective_dimension_metadata_for_lang(normalized)
   402→
   403→
   404→def get_dimension_metadata(
   405→    dimension_name: str, *, lang_name: str | None = None
   406→) -> dict[str, object]:
   407→    """Return metadata for one dimension key (with sane defaults)."""
   408→    dim = _normalize_dimension_name(dimension_name)
   409→    all_meta = _metadata_registry(lang_name)
   410→    payload = dict(all_meta.get(dim, {}))
   411→
   412→    payload.setdefault("display_name", _title_display_name(dim))
   413→    payload.setdefault("weight", 1.0)
   414→    payload.setdefault("enabled_by_default", False)
   415→    payload.setdefault("reset_on_scan", True)
   416→    return payload
   417→
   418→
   419→def dimension_display_name(dimension_name: str, *, lang_name: str | None = None) -> str:
   420→    meta = get_dimension_metadata(dimension_name, lang_name=lang_name)
   421→    return str(meta.get("display_name", _title_display_name(dimension_name)))
   422→
   423→
   424→def dimension_weight(dimension_name: str, *, lang_name: str | None = None) -> float:
   425→    meta = get_dimension_metadata(dimension_name, lang_name=lang_name)
   426→    raw = meta.get("weight", 1.0)
   427→    if is_numeric(raw):
   428→        return max(0.0, float(raw))
   429→    return 1.0
   430→
   431→
   432→def default_display_names_map(*, lang_name: str | None = None) -> dict[str, str]:
   433→    """Display-name map for default subjective dimensions."""
   434→    out: dict[str, str] = {}
   435→    for dim, payload in _metadata_registry(lang_name).items():
   436→        if not bool(payload.get("enabled_by_default", False)):
   437→            continue
   438→        out[dim] = str(payload.get("display_name", _title_display_name(dim)))
   439→    return out
   440→
   441→
   442→def resettable_default_dimensions(*, lang_name: str | None = None) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   443→    """Default subjective dimensions that should be reset by scan reset."""
   444→    out = []
   445→    for dim, payload in _metadata_registry(lang_name).items():
   446→        if not bool(payload.get("enabled_by_default", False)):
   447→            continue
   448→        if not bool(payload.get("reset_on_scan", True)):
   449→            continue
   450→        out.append(dim)
   451→    return tuple(sorted(set(out)))
   452→
   453→
   454→__all__ = [
   455→    "DISPLAY_NAMES",
   456→    "configure_subjective_dimension_providers",
   457→    "default_dimension_keys",
   458→    "default_dimension_keys_for_lang",
   459→    "default_display_names_map",
   460→    "dimension_display_name",
   461→    "dimension_weight",
   462→    "get_dimension_metadata",
   463→    "load_subjective_dimension_metadata",
   464→    "load_subjective_dimension_metadata_for_lang",
   465→    "reset_subjective_dimension_providers",
   466→    "resettable_default_dimensions",
   467→]

    29→def path_scoped_issues(
    30→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→) -> dict[str, Issue]:
    33→    """Filter issues to those within the given scan path."""
    34→    return {
    35→        issue_id: issue
    36→        for issue_id, issue in issues.items()
    37→        if issue_in_scan_scope(str(issue.get("file", "")), scan_path)
    38→    }
    39→
    40→
    41→def issue_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    42→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    43→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    44→        return True
    45→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    46→    return (
    47→        file_path.startswith(prefix)
    48→        or file_path == scan_path
    49→        or file_path == "."
    50→    )
    51→
    52→
    53→def open_scope_breakdown(
    54→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    55→    scan_path: str | None,
    56→    *,
    57→    detector: str | None = None,
    58→) -> dict[str, int]:
    59→    """Return open-issue counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
    60→    in_scope = 0
    61→    out_of_scope = 0
    62→
    63→    for issue in issues.values():
    64→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
    65→            continue
    66→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
    67→            continue
    68→        if detector is not None and issue.get("detector") != detector:
    69→            continue
    70→        file_path = str(issue.get("file", ""))
    71→        if issue_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
    72→            in_scope += 1
    73→        else:
    74→            out_of_scope += 1
    75→
    76→    return {
    77→        "in_scope": in_scope,
    78→        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
    79→        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
    80→    }

    11→    current_by_detector: dict[str, int],
    12→    force_resolve: bool,
    13→    ran_detectors: set[str] | None = None,
    14→) -> set[str]:
    15→    """Detectors that had open issues but likely did not actually run this scan."""
    16→    if force_resolve:
    17→        return set()
    18→
    19→    previous_open_by_detector: dict[str, int] = {}
    20→    for issue in existing.values():
    21→        if issue["status"] != "open":
    22→            continue
    23→        detector = issue.get("detector", "unknown")
    24→        previous_open_by_detector[detector] = (
    25→            previous_open_by_detector.get(detector, 0) + 1
    26→        )
    27→
    28→    # 'review' issues enter via `desloppify review --import`, not via scan phases.
    29→    # They are always marked suspect so the scan never auto-resolves them.
    30→    import_only_detectors = {"review"}
    31→    suspect: set[str] = set()
    32→

    34→        if detector in import_only_detectors:

    38→            continue
    39→        if ran_detectors is not None:
    40→            if detector not in ran_detectors:
    41→                suspect.add(detector)
    42→            continue
    43→        if previous_count >= 3:
    44→            suspect.add(detector)
    45→
    46→    return suspect

   124→def triage_phase_banner(plan: PlanModel) -> str:
   125→    """Return a banner string when triage stage IDs are in the queue."""
   126→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   127→    order = set(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   128→    has_triage = any(sid in order for sid in TRIAGE_IDS)
   129→    if not has_triage:
   130→        return ""
   131→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta", {})
   132→    stages = meta.get("triage_stages", {})
   133→    completed = [s for s in ("observe", "reflect", "organize") if s in stages]
   134→    if completed:
   135→        return (
   136→            f"TRIAGE MODE ({len(completed)}/4 stages complete) — "
   137→            "complete all stages to exit. Run: desloppify plan triage"
   138→        )
   139→    return (
   140→        "TRIAGE MODE — review issues need analysis before fixing. "
   141→        "Run: desloppify plan triage"
   142→    )
   143→

1→"""Text-oriented utility helpers split from the main utils facade."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.runtime_state import current_runtime_context
     9→
    10→_DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT = Path(os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_ROOT", Path.cwd())).resolve()
    11→
    12→
    13→def get_project_root() -> Path:
    14→    """Return the active project root, checking RuntimeContext first.
    15→
    16→    Tests set ``RuntimeContext.project_root`` to point at a tmp directory.
    17→    Production code uses the process-level default from $DESLOPPIFY_ROOT / cwd.
    18→    """
    19→    override = current_runtime_context().project_root
    20→    if override is not None:
    21→        return override
    22→    return _DEFAULT_PROJECT_ROOT
    23→
    24→
    25→def read_code_snippet(
    26→    filepath: str,
    27→    line: int,
    28→    context: int = 1,
    29→    *,
    30→    project_root: Path | str | None = None,
    31→) -> str | None:
    32→    """Read ±context lines around a line number. Returns formatted string or None."""
    33→    try:
    34→        root = (
    35→            Path(project_root).resolve()
    36→            if project_root is not None
    37→            # Runtime-aware default; preserves RuntimeContext/project-root overrides.
    38→            else get_project_root()
    39→        )
    40→        full = Path(filepath)
    41→        if not full.is_absolute():
    42→            full = root / full
    43→        content = full.read_text(errors="replace")
    44→    except OSError:
    45→        return None
    46→    lines = content.splitlines()
    47→    if line < 1 or line > len(lines):
    48→        return None
    49→    start = max(0, line - 1 - context)
    50→    end = min(len(lines), line + context)
    51→    parts = []
    52→    for i in range(start, end):
    53→        ln = i + 1
    54→        marker = "→" if ln == line else " "
    55→        text = lines[i]
    56→        if len(text) > 120:
    57→            text = text[:117] + "..."
    58→        parts.append(f"    {marker} {ln:>4} │ {text}")
    59→    return "\n".join(parts)
    60→
    61→
    62→def get_area(filepath: str, *, min_depth: int = 2) -> str:
    63→    """Derive an area name from a file path (generic: first 2 path components).
    64→
    65→    *min_depth* controls the minimum number of path components required before
    66→    the two-component area is returned.  The default (2) means a path like
    67→    ``"a/b"`` already qualifies.  Python uses ``min_depth=3`` so that
    68→    ``"pkg/mod.py"`` returns just ``"pkg"`` while ``"pkg/sub/mod.py"`` returns
    69→    ``"pkg/sub"``.
    70→    """
    71→    text = (filepath or "").strip()
    72→    if not text:
    73→        return "(unknown)"
    74→    parts = Path(text).parts
    75→    if not parts:
    76→        return "(unknown)"
    77→    return "/".join(parts[:2]) if len(parts) >= min_depth else parts[0]
    78→
    79→
    80→def _consume_escaped_char(text: str, index: int, out: list[str]) -> int | None:
    81→    if text[index] != "\\" or index + 1 >= len(text):
    82→        return None
    83→    out.append(text[index : index + 2])
    84→    return index + 2
    85→
    86→
    87→def _skip_comment(text: str, index: int) -> int | None:
    88→    if text[index] != "/" or index + 1 >= len(text):
    89→        return None
    90→    next_char = text[index + 1]
    91→    if next_char == "/":
    92→        newline_at = text.find("\n", index)
    93→        return -1 if newline_at == -1 else newline_at
    94→    if next_char == "*":
    95→        end = text.find("*/", index + 2)
    96→        return -1 if end == -1 else end + 2
    97→    return None
    98→
    99→
   100→def strip_c_style_comments(text: str) -> str:
   101→    """Strip // and /* */ comments while preserving string literals."""
   102→    result: list[str] = []
   103→    i = 0
   104→    in_str = None
   105→    while i < len(text):
   106→        ch = text[i]
   107→        if in_str:
   108→            escaped_next = _consume_escaped_char(text, i, result)
   109→            if escaped_next is not None:
   110→                i = escaped_next
   111→                continue
   112→            if ch == in_str:
   113→                in_str = None
   114→            result.append(ch)
   115→            i += 1
   116→            continue
   117→
   118→        if ch in ('"', "'", "`"):
   119→            in_str = ch
   120→            result.append(ch)
   121→            i += 1
   122→            continue
   123→
   124→        skipped_to = _skip_comment(text, i)
   125→        if skipped_to is not None:
   126→            if skipped_to == -1:
   127→                break
   128→            i = skipped_to
   129→            continue
   130→
   131→        result.append(ch)
   132→        i += 1
   133→    return "".join(result)
   134→
   135→
   136→def is_numeric(value: object) -> bool:
   137→    """Return True if *value* is an int or float but NOT a bool.
   138→
   139→    Python's ``bool`` is a subclass of ``int``, so ``isinstance(True, int)``
   140→    is ``True``.  Many JSON-derived payloads need to distinguish real numbers
   141→    from booleans; this helper centralises that guard.
   142→    """
   143→    return isinstance(value, int | float) and not isinstance(value, bool)
   144→
   145→
   146→__all__ = [
   147→    "get_area",
   148→    "get_project_root",
   149→    "is_numeric",
   150→    "read_code_snippet",
   151→    "strip_c_style_comments",
   152→]
   153→

     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.annotations import get_issue_note

    30→
    31→    superseded: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    32→    pruned: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)

    36→
    37→def _find_candidates(
    38→    state: StateModel, detector: str, file: str
    39→) -> list[str]:
    40→    """Find open issues that could be remaps for a disappeared issue."""
    41→    candidates: list[str] = []
    42→    for fid, issue in state.get("issues", {}).items():
    43→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
    44→            continue
    45→        if issue.get("detector") == detector and issue.get("file") == file:
    46→            candidates.append(fid)
    47→    return candidates
    48→
    49→
    50→def _is_issue_alive(state: StateModel, issue_id: str) -> bool:
    51→    """Return True if the issue exists and is open."""
    52→    issue = state.get("issues", {}).get(issue_id)
    53→    if issue is None:
    54→        return False
    55→    return issue.get("status") == "open"
    56→
    57→
    58→def _supersede_id(
    59→    plan: PlanModel,
    60→    state: StateModel,
    61→    issue_id: str,
    62→    now: str,
    63→) -> bool:
    64→    """Move a disappeared issue to superseded. Returns True if changed."""
    65→    issue = state.get("issues", {}).get(issue_id)
    66→    detector = ""
    67→    file = ""
    68→    summary = ""
    69→    if issue:
    70→        detector = issue.get("detector", "")
    71→        file = issue.get("file", "")
    72→        summary = issue.get("summary", "")
    73→
    74→    candidates = _find_candidates(state, detector, file) if detector else []
    75→    # Don't include the original in candidates
    76→    candidates = [c for c in candidates if c != issue_id]
    77→
    78→    entry: SupersededEntry = {
    79→        "original_id": issue_id,
    80→        "original_detector": detector,
    81→        "original_file": file,
    82→        "original_summary": summary,
    83→        "status": "superseded",
    84→        "superseded_at": now,
    85→        "remapped_to": None,
    86→        "candidates": candidates[:5],
    87→    }
    88→
    89→    # Preserve any existing override note
    90→    override_note = get_issue_note(plan, issue_id)
    91→    if override_note:
    92→        entry["note"] = override_note
    93→
    94→    plan["superseded"][issue_id] = entry
    95→
    96→    # Remove from queue_order, skipped, promoted_ids, cluster issue_ids
    97→    order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    98→    skipped: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    99→    if issue_id in order:
   100→        order.remove(issue_id)
   101→    skipped.pop(issue_id, None)
   102→    prune_promoted_ids(plan, {issue_id})
   103→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   104→        ids = cluster.get("issue_ids", [])
   105→        if issue_id in ids:
   106→            ids.remove(issue_id)
   107→
   108→    # Clear stale cluster reference from override
   109→    override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(issue_id)
   110→    if override and override.get("cluster"):
   111→        override["cluster"] = None
   112→        override["updated_at"] = now
   113→
   114→    return True
   115→
   116→
   117→def _prune_old_superseded(plan: PlanModel, now_dt: datetime) -> list[str]:
   118→    """Remove superseded entries older than TTL. Returns pruned IDs."""
   119→    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})
   120→    cutoff = now_dt - timedelta(days=SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS)

1→"""Concern generators — mechanical issues → subjective review bridge.
     2→
     3→Concerns are ephemeral: computed on-demand from current state, never persisted.
     4→Only LLM-confirmed concerns become persistent Issue objects via review import.
     5→
     6→Generators focus on cross-cutting synthesis — bundling all signals per file so
     7→the LLM gets a complete picture, and surfacing systemic patterns across files
     8→that no single detector captures.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import hashlib
    14→import re
    15→from collections import defaultdict
    16→from dataclasses import dataclass
    17→from typing import Any, Literal, TypedDict
    18→
    19→from desloppify.base.registry import JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    20→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    21→from desloppify.engine.detectors.base import (
    22→    ELEVATED_LOC_THRESHOLD,
    23→    ELEVATED_NESTING_THRESHOLD,
    24→    ELEVATED_PARAMS_THRESHOLD,
    25→)
    26→
    27→# ── Concern thresholds ──────────────────────────────────
    28→ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS = ELEVATED_PARAMS_THRESHOLD
    29→ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING = ELEVATED_NESTING_THRESHOLD
    30→ELEVATED_LOC = ELEVATED_LOC_THRESHOLD
    31→MIN_DETECTORS_FOR_MIXED = 3
    32→MIN_FILES_FOR_SYSTEMIC = 3
    33→MIN_FILES_FOR_SMELL_PATTERN = 5
    34→
    35→
    36→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    37→class Concern:
    38→    """A potential design problem surfaced by mechanical signals."""
    39→
    40→    type: str  # concern classification
    41→    file: str  # primary file (relative path)
    42→    summary: str  # human-readable 1-liner
    43→    evidence: tuple[str, ...]  # supporting data points
    44→    question: str  # specific question for LLM to evaluate
    45→    fingerprint: str  # stable hash for dismissal tracking
    46→    source_issues: tuple[str, ...]  # issue IDs that triggered this
    47→
    48→
    49→class ConcernSignals(TypedDict, total=False):
    50→    """Typed signal payload extracted from mechanical issues."""
    51→
    52→    max_params: float
    53→    max_nesting: float
    54→    loc: float
    55→    function_count: float
    56→    monster_loc: float
    57→    monster_funcs: list[str]
    58→
    59→
    60→SignalKey = Literal["max_params", "max_nesting", "loc", "function_count", "monster_loc"]
    61→
    62→
    63→def _update_max_signal(signals: ConcernSignals, key: SignalKey, value: object) -> None:
    64→    """Update numeric signal key with max(existing, value) when value is valid."""
    65→    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
    66→        return
    67→    current = float(signals.get(key, 0.0))
    68→    signals[key] = max(current, float(value))
    69→
    70→
    71→def _fingerprint(concern_type: str, file: str, key_signals: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    72→    """Stable hash of (type, file, sorted key signals)."""
    73→    raw = f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"
    74→    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    75→
    76→
    77→def _is_dismissed(
    78→    dismissals: dict, fingerprint: str, source_issue_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    79→) -> bool:
    80→    """Check if a concern was previously dismissed and source issues unchanged."""
    81→    entry = dismissals.get(fingerprint)
    82→    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    83→        return False
    84→    prev_sources = set(entry.get("source_issue_ids", []))
    85→    return prev_sources == set(source_issue_ids)
    86→
    87→
    88→def _open_issues(state: StateModel) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    89→    """Return all open issues from state."""
    90→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    91→    return [
    92→        f for f in issues.values()
    93→        if isinstance(f, dict) and f.get("status") == "open"
    94→    ]
    95→
    96→
    97→def _group_by_file(state: StateModel) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:
    98→    """Group open issues by file, excluding holistic (file='.')."""
    99→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
   100→    for f in _open_issues(state):
   101→        file = f.get("file", "")
   102→        if file and file != ".":
   103→            by_file[file].append(f)
   104→    return dict(by_file)
   105→
   106→
   107→# ── Signal extraction ────────────────────────────────────────────────
   108→
   109→
   110→def _parse_complexity_signals(detail: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, float]:
   111→    """Parse complexity_signals strings into numeric values.
   112→
   113→    Structural detail dicts store complexity signals as strings like
   114→    "12 params" and "nesting depth 8" in the ``complexity_signals``
   115→    list.  Extract max_params and max_nesting from those labels so
   116→    the concern generator can evaluate thresholds.
   117→    """
   118→    result: dict[str, float] = {}
   119→    raw_signals = detail.get("complexity_signals", [])
   120→    if not isinstance(raw_signals, list):
   121→        return result
   122→
   123→    for sig in raw_signals:
   124→        if not isinstance(sig, str):
   125→            continue
   126→        m = re.search(r"(\d+)\s*params", sig)
   127→        if m:
   128→            result["max_params"] = max(result.get("max_params", 0), float(m.group(1)))
   129→        m = re.search(r"nesting depth\s*(\d+)", sig)
   130→        if m:
   131→            result["max_nesting"] = max(result.get("max_nesting", 0), float(m.group(1)))
   132→    return result
   133→
   134→
   135→def _extract_signals(issues: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ConcernSignals:
   136→    """Extract key quantitative signals from a file's issues."""
   137→    signals: ConcernSignals = {}
   138→    monster_funcs: list[str] = []
   139→
   140→    for f in issues:
   141→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   142→        detail_raw = f.get("detail", {})
   143→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
   144→
   145→        if det == "structural":
   146→            # Read loc directly from the flat detail dict.
   147→            _update_max_signal(signals, "loc", detail.get("loc", 0))
   148→            # Parse complexity_signals strings for params/nesting.
   149→            parsed = _parse_complexity_signals(detail)
   150→            for key in ("max_params", "max_nesting"):
   151→                if key in parsed:
   152→                    _update_max_signal(signals, key, parsed[key])
   153→
   154→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   155→            _update_max_signal(signals, "monster_loc", detail.get("loc", 0))
   156→            func = detail.get("function", "")
   157→            if isinstance(func, str) and func:
   158→                monster_funcs.append(func)
   159→
   160→    if monster_funcs:
   161→        signals["monster_funcs"] = monster_funcs
   162→    return signals
   163→
   164→
   165→def _has_elevated_signals(issues: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> bool:
   166→    """Does any issue have signals strong enough to flag on its own?"""
   167→    for f in issues:
   168→        det = f.get("detector", "")
   169→        detail_raw = f.get("detail", {})
   170→        detail = detail_raw if isinstance(detail_raw, dict) else {}
   171→
   172→        if det == "structural":
   173→            # Check flat detail keys first (real structural data).
   174→            if detail.get("loc", 0) >= ELEVATED_LOC:
   175→                return True
   176→            # Parse complexity_signals strings.
   177→            parsed = _parse_complexity_signals(detail)
   178→            if parsed.get("max_params", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
   179→                return True
   180→            if parsed.get("max_nesting", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
   181→                return True
   182→
   183→        if det == "smells" and detail.get("smell_id") == "monster_function":
   184→            return True
   185→
   186→        if det in ("dupes", "boilerplate_duplication", "coupling",
   187→                    "responsibility_cohesion"):
   188→            return True
   189→
   190→    return False
   191→
   192→
   193→# ── Concern classification ───────────────────────────────────────────
   194→
   195→
   196→def _classify(detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals) -> str:
   197→    """Pick the most specific concern type from what's present."""
   198→    if len(detectors) >= MIN_DETECTORS_FOR_MIXED:
   199→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
   200→    if "dupes" in detectors or "boilerplate_duplication" in detectors:
   201→        return "duplication_design"
   202→    if signals.get("monster_loc", 0) > 0:
   203→        return "structural_complexity"
   204→    if "coupling" in detectors:
   205→        return "coupling_design"
   206→    if signals.get("max_params", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
   207→        return "interface_design"
   208→    if signals.get("max_nesting", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
   209→        return "structural_complexity"
   210→    if "responsibility_cohesion" in detectors:
   211→        return "mixed_responsibilities"
   212→    return "design_concern"
   213→
   214→
   215→def _build_summary(
   216→    concern_type: str,
   217→    detectors: set[str],
   218→    signals: ConcernSignals,
   219→) -> str:
   220→    """Human-readable one-liner."""
   221→    if concern_type == "mixed_responsibilities":
   222→        return (
   223→            f"Issues from {len(detectors)} detectors — "
   224→            "may have too many responsibilities"
   225→        )
   226→    if concern_type == "structural_complexity":
   227→        parts: list[str] = []
   228→        monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc", 0)
   229→        if monster_loc:
   230→            funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   231→            label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
   232→            parts.append(f"monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
   233→        nesting = signals.get("max_nesting", 0)
   234→        if nesting >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
   235→            parts.append(f"nesting depth {int(nesting)}")
   236→        params = signals.get("max_params", 0)
   237→        if params >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
   238→            parts.append(f"{int(params)} parameters")
   239→        return f"Structural complexity: {', '.join(parts) or 'elevated signals'}"
   240→    if concern_type == "duplication_design":
   241→        return "Duplication pattern — assess if extraction is warranted"
   242→    if concern_type == "coupling_design":
   243→        return "Coupling pattern — assess if boundaries need adjustment"
   244→    if concern_type == "interface_design":
   245→        return f"Interface complexity: {int(signals.get('max_params', 0))} parameters"
   246→    return f"Design signals from {', '.join(sorted(detectors))}"
   247→
   248→
   249→def _build_evidence(
   250→    issues: list[dict[str, Any]],
   251→    signals: ConcernSignals,
   252→) -> tuple[str, ...]:
   253→    """Build evidence tuple from all issues and extracted signals."""
   254→    evidence: list[str] = []
   255→
   256→    detectors = sorted({f.get("detector", "") for f in issues})
   257→    evidence.append(f"Flagged by: {', '.join(detectors)}")
   258→
   259→    loc = signals.get("loc")
   260→    if loc:
   261→        evidence.append(f"File size: {int(loc)} lines")
   262→    params = signals.get("max_params")
   263→    if params and params >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
   264→        evidence.append(f"Max parameters: {int(params)}")
   265→    nesting = signals.get("max_nesting")
   266→    if nesting and nesting >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
   267→        evidence.append(f"Max nesting depth: {int(nesting)}")
   268→    monster_loc = signals.get("monster_loc")
   269→    if monster_loc:
   270→        funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   271→        label = f" ({', '.join(funcs[:3])})" if funcs else ""
   272→        evidence.append(f"Monster function{label}: {int(monster_loc)} lines")
   273→
   274→    # Individual issue summaries — give LLM the full picture, capped.
   275→    for f in issues[:10]:
   276→        summary = f.get("summary", "")
   277→        if summary:
   278→            evidence.append(f"[{f.get('detector', '')}] {summary}")
   279→
   280→    return tuple(evidence)
   281→
   282→
   283→def _build_question(
   284→    detectors: set[str], signals: ConcernSignals
   285→) -> str:
   286→    """Build targeted question from dominant signals."""
   287→    parts: list[str] = []
   288→
   289→    if len(detectors) >= MIN_DETECTORS_FOR_MIXED:
   290→        parts.append(
   291→            f"This file has issues across {len(detectors)} dimensions "
   292→            f"({', '.join(sorted(detectors))}). Is it trying to do too many "
   293→            "things, or is this complexity inherent to its domain?"
   294→        )
   295→
   296→    funcs = signals.get("monster_funcs", [])
   297→    if funcs:
   298→        parts.append(
   299→            f"What are the distinct responsibilities in {funcs[0]}()? "
   300→            "Should it be decomposed into focused functions?"
   301→        )
   302→
   303→    if signals.get("max_params", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_PARAMS:
   304→        parts.append(
   305→            "Should the parameters be grouped into a config/context object? "
   306→            "Which ones belong together?"
   307→        )
   308→
   309→    if signals.get("max_nesting", 0) >= ELEVATED_MAX_NESTING:
   310→        parts.append(
   311→            "Can the nesting be reduced with early returns, guard clauses, "
   312→            "or extraction into helper functions?"
   313→        )
   314→
   315→    if "dupes" in detectors or "boilerplate_duplication" in detectors:
   316→        parts.append(
   317→            "Is the duplication worth extracting into a shared utility, "
   318→            "or is it intentional variation?"
   319→        )
   320→
   321→    if "coupling" in detectors:
   322→        parts.append(
   323→            "Is the coupling intentional or does it indicate a missing "
   324→            "abstraction boundary?"
   325→        )
   326→
   327→    if "orphaned" in detectors:
   328→        parts.append(
   329→            "Is this file truly dead, or is it used via a non-import mechanism "
   330→            "(dynamic import, CLI entry point, plugin)?"
   331→        )
   332→
   333→    if "responsibility_cohesion" in detectors:
   334→        parts.append(
   335→            "What are the distinct responsibilities? Should this module be "
   336→            "split along those lines?"
   337→        )
   338→
   339→    if not parts:
   340→        parts.append(
   341→            "Review the flagged patterns — are they design problems that "
   342→            "need addressing, or acceptable given the file's role?"
   343→        )
   344→
   345→    return " ".join(parts)
   346→
   347→
   348→# ── Generators ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   349→
   350→
   351→def _file_concerns(state: StateModel, dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
   352→    """Per-file design concerns from aggregated mechanical signals.
   353→
   354→    Flags a file if it has 2+ judgment-needed detectors OR a single
   355→    detector with elevated signals (monster function, high params,
   356→    deep nesting, duplication, coupling, mixed responsibilities).
   357→    Bundles ALL issues for that file so the LLM sees the full picture.
   358→    """
   359→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
   360→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   361→
   362→    for file, all_issues in by_file.items():
   363→        judgment = [
   364→            f for f in all_issues
   365→            if f.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   366→        ]
   367→        if not judgment:
   368→            continue
   369→
   370→        judgment_dets = {f.get("detector", "") for f in judgment}
   371→        elevated = _has_elevated_signals(judgment)
   372→
   373→        # Flag if 2+ judgment detectors OR 1 with elevated signals
   374→        # OR 1 judgment detector + 2 mechanical issues from any detector.
   375→        mechanical_count = len(all_issues)
   376→        if len(judgment_dets) < 2 and not elevated:
   377→            if not (len(judgment_dets) >= 1 and mechanical_count >= 3):
   378→                continue
   379→
   380→        signals = _extract_signals(judgment)
   381→        concern_type = _classify(judgment_dets, signals)
   382→        evidence = _build_evidence(judgment, signals)
   383→        question = _build_question(judgment_dets, signals)
   384→        summary = _build_summary(concern_type, judgment_dets, signals)
   385→
   386→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(f.get("id", "") for f in judgment))
   387→        fp_keys = tuple(sorted(judgment_dets))
   388→        fp = _fingerprint(concern_type, file, fp_keys)
   389→
   390→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   391→            continue
   392→
   393→        concerns.append(
   394→            Concern(
   395→                type=concern_type,
   396→                file=file,
   397→                summary=summary,
   398→                evidence=evidence,
   399→                question=question,
   400→                fingerprint=fp,
   401→                source_issues=all_ids,
   402→            )
   403→        )
   404→
   405→    return concerns
   406→
   407→
   408→def _cross_file_patterns(state: StateModel, dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
   409→    """Systemic patterns: same judgment detector combo across 3+ files.
   410→
   411→    When multiple files share the same combination of detector types,
   412→    that's likely a codebase-wide pattern rather than isolated issues.
   413→    """
   414→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
   415→
   416→    # Group files by their judgment detector profile.
   417→    profile_to_files: dict[frozenset[str], list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   418→    for file, issues in by_file.items():
   419→        dets = frozenset(
   420→            f.get("detector", "") for f in issues
   421→            if f.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
   422→        )
   423→        if len(dets) >= 2:
   424→            profile_to_files[dets].append(file)
   425→
   426→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   427→    for det_combo, files in profile_to_files.items():
   428→        if len(files) < MIN_FILES_FOR_SYSTEMIC:
   429→            continue
   430→
   431→        sorted_files = sorted(files)
   432→        combo_names = tuple(sorted(det_combo))
   433→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(
   434→            f.get("id", "")
   435→            for file in sorted_files
   436→            for f in by_file[file]
   437→            if f.get("detector", "") in det_combo
   438→        ))
   439→        # Use first few files in fingerprint so it's stable but bounded.
   440→        fp = _fingerprint(
   441→            "systemic_pattern",
   442→            ",".join(sorted_files[:5]),
   443→            combo_names,
   444→        )
   445→
   446→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   447→            continue
   448→
   449→        concerns.append(
   450→            Concern(
   451→                type="systemic_pattern",
   452→                file=sorted_files[0],
   453→                summary=(
   454→                    f"{len(files)} files share the same problem pattern "
   455→                    f"({', '.join(combo_names)})"
   456→                ),
   457→                evidence=(
   458→                    f"Affected files: {', '.join(sorted_files[:10])}",
   459→                    f"Shared detectors: {', '.join(combo_names)}",
   460→                    f"Total files: {len(files)}",
   461→                ),
   462→                question=(
   463→                    f"These {len(files)} files all have the same combination "
   464→                    f"of issues ({', '.join(combo_names)}). Is this a systemic "
   465→                    "pattern that should be addressed at the architecture level "
   466→                    "(shared base class, framework change, lint rule), or are "
   467→                    "these independent issues that happen to look similar?"
   468→                ),
   469→                fingerprint=fp,
   470→                source_issues=all_ids,
   471→            )
   472→        )
   473→
   474→    return concerns
   475→
   476→
   477→def _systemic_smell_patterns(
   478→    state: StateModel, dismissals: dict[str, Any]
   479→) -> list[Concern]:
   480→    """Systemic concerns: single smell_id appearing across 5+ files.
   481→
   482→    Complements _cross_file_patterns which looks at detector-combo profiles.
   483→    This catches pervasive single-smell issues (e.g. broad_except in 12 files).
   484→    """
   485→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   486→    smell_ids_map: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)  # smell_id -> issue IDs
   487→
   488→    for f in _open_issues(state):
   489→        if f.get("detector") != "smells":
   490→            continue
   491→        detail = f.get("detail", {})
   492→        smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
   493→        filepath = f.get("file", "")
   494→        if smell_id and filepath and filepath != ".":
   495→            smell_files[smell_id].append(filepath)
   496→            smell_ids_map[smell_id].append(f.get("id", ""))
   497→
   498→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   499→    for smell_id, files in smell_files.items():
   500→        unique_files = sorted(set(files))
   501→        if len(unique_files) < MIN_FILES_FOR_SMELL_PATTERN:
   502→            continue
   503→
   504→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(smell_ids_map[smell_id]))
   505→        fp = _fingerprint("systemic_smell", smell_id, (smell_id,))
   506→        if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
   507→            continue
   508→
   509→        concerns.append(
   510→            Concern(
   511→                type="systemic_smell",
   512→                file=unique_files[0],
   513→                summary=(
   514→                    f"'{smell_id}' appears in {len(unique_files)} files — "
   515→                    "likely a systemic pattern"
   516→                ),
   517→                evidence=(
   518→                    f"Smell: {smell_id}",
   519→                    f"Affected files ({len(unique_files)}): {', '.join(unique_files[:10])}",
   520→                ),
   521→                question=(
   522→                    f"The smell '{smell_id}' appears across {len(unique_files)} files. "
   523→                    "Is this a codebase-wide convention that should be addressed "
   524→                    "systemically (lint rule, shared utility, architecture change), "
   525→                    "or are these independent occurrences?"
   526→                ),
   527→                fingerprint=fp,
   528→                source_issues=all_ids,
   529→            )
   530→        )
   531→
   532→    return concerns
   533→
   534→
   535→_GENERATORS = [_file_concerns, _cross_file_patterns, _systemic_smell_patterns]
   536→
   537→
   538→def generate_concerns(
   539→    state: StateModel,
   540→) -> list[Concern]:
   541→    """Run all concern generators against current state.
   542→
   543→    Returns deduplicated list sorted by (type, file).
   544→    """
   545→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   546→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   547→    seen_fps: set[str] = set()
   548→
   549→    for gen in _GENERATORS:
   550→        for concern in gen(state, dismissals):
   551→            if concern.fingerprint not in seen_fps:
   552→                seen_fps.add(concern.fingerprint)
   553→                concerns.append(concern)
   554→
   555→    concerns.sort(key=lambda c: (c.type, c.file))
   556→    return concerns
   557→
   558→
   559→def cleanup_stale_dismissals(state: StateModel) -> int:
   560→    """Remove dismissals whose source issues all disappeared.
   561→
   562→    Returns the number of stale entries removed.  Dismissals without
   563→    ``source_issue_ids`` (legacy) are left untouched.
   564→    """
   565→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   566→    if not dismissals:
   567→        return 0
   568→    open_ids = {f.get("id", "") for f in _open_issues(state)}
   569→    stale_fps = [
   570→        fp
   571→        for fp, entry in dismissals.items()
   572→        if entry.get("source_issue_ids")
   573→        and not any(sid in open_ids for sid in entry["source_issue_ids"])
   574→    ]
   575→    for fp in stale_fps:
   576→        del dismissals[fp]
   577→    return len(stale_fps)
   578→
   579→
   580→__all__ = ["Concern", "cleanup_stale_dismissals", "generate_concerns"]
   581→

1→"""Language-resolution helpers for command modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import logging
     6→from collections.abc import Callable
     7→from functools import lru_cache
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
    10→
    11→from desloppify import languages as lang_api
    12→from desloppify.base.text.text_api import get_project_root
    13→
    14→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    15→    from desloppify.languages._framework.base.types import LangConfig
    16→
    17→
    18→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    19→
    20→
    21→class LangResolutionError(SystemExit):
    22→    """Raised when language resolution fails with a user-facing message.
    23→
    24→    Inherits from SystemExit so that callers which don't catch it explicitly
    25→    will still terminate with a non-zero exit code, matching the previous
    26→    sys.exit(1) behaviour.  The CLI top-level catches it to print a clean
    27→    error without a traceback.
    28→    """
    29→
    30→    def __init__(self, message: str) -> None:
    31→        self.message = message
    32→        super().__init__(1)
    33→
    34→
    35→EXTRA_ROOT_MARKERS = (
    36→    "package.json",
    37→    "pyproject.toml",
    38→    "setup.py",
    39→    "setup.cfg",
    40→    "go.mod",
    41→    "Cargo.toml",
    42→)
    43→
    44→
    45→def _cache_once(fn: Callable[[], tuple[str, ...]]) -> Callable[[], tuple[str, ...]]:
    46→    """Cache a zero-arg function once while preserving cache_clear for tests."""
    47→    cached = lru_cache(maxsize=1)(fn)
    48→    return cached
    49→
    50→
    51→@_cache_once
    52→def _lang_config_markers() -> tuple[str, ...]:
    53→    """Collect project-root marker files from language plugins + fallback markers."""
    54→    markers = set(EXTRA_ROOT_MARKERS)
    55→
    56→    for lang_name in lang_api.available_langs():
    57→        try:
    58→            cfg = lang_api.get_lang(lang_name)
    59→        except (ImportError, ValueError, TypeError, AttributeError) as exc:
    60→            logger.debug(
    61→                "Skipping language marker collection for %s: %s", lang_name, exc
    62→            )
    63→            continue
    64→        for marker in getattr(cfg, "detect_markers", []) or []:
    65→            if not isinstance(marker, str):
    66→                continue
    67→            cleaned = marker.strip()
    68→            if cleaned:
    69→                markers.add(cleaned)
    70→    return tuple(sorted(markers))
    71→
    72→
    73→def resolve_detection_root(
    74→    args,
    75→    *,
    76→    project_root: Path | None = None,
    77→    marker_provider: Callable[[], tuple[str, ...]] | None = None,
    78→) -> Path:
    79→    """Best root to auto-detect language from."""
    80→    marker_provider = marker_provider or _lang_config_markers

    12→

    15→    line: int

    23→
    24→
    25→@dataclass
    26→class ClassInfo:
    27→    """Extracted class/component info for cross-language analysis.
    28→
    29→    For OOP classes: methods/attributes/base_classes are populated directly.
    30→    For React components: metrics holds hook counts (context_hooks, use_effects, etc.).

1→"""Scan metadata/history helpers for merge operations."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.base.tooling import compute_tool_hash
     6→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import ScanDiff, StateModel
     7→
     8→
     9→def _record_scan_metadata(
    10→    state: StateModel,
    11→    now: str,
    12→    *,
    13→    lang: str | None,
    14→    include_slow: bool,
    15→    scan_path: str | None,
    16→) -> None:
    17→    state["last_scan"] = now
    18→    state["scan_count"] = state.get("scan_count", 0) + 1
    19→    state["tool_hash"] = compute_tool_hash()
    20→    state["scan_path"] = scan_path
    21→    if lang:
    22→        state.setdefault("scan_completeness", {})[lang] = (
    23→            "full" if include_slow else "fast"
    24→        )
    25→
    26→
    27→def _merge_scan_inputs(
    28→    state: StateModel,
    29→    *,
    30→    lang: str | None,
    31→    potentials: dict[str, int] | None,
    32→    merge_potentials: bool,
    33→    codebase_metrics: dict | None,
    34→) -> None:
    35→    if potentials is not None and lang:
    36→        all_potentials = state.setdefault("potentials", {})
    37→        if merge_potentials and isinstance(all_potentials.get(lang), dict):
    38→            merged = dict(all_potentials[lang])
    39→            merged.update(potentials)
    40→            all_potentials[lang] = merged
    41→        else:
    42→            all_potentials[lang] = dict(potentials)
    43→
    44→    if codebase_metrics is not None and lang:
    45→        state.setdefault("codebase_metrics", {})[lang] = dict(codebase_metrics)
    46→
    47→

    50→

    55→    return {

    57→        "matched_count": int(integrity.get("matched_count", 0) or 0),
    58→        "reset_count": len(

1→"""Runtime context helpers for command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from pathlib import Path
     7→from typing import Any
     8→
     9→from desloppify import state as state_mod
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    11→from desloppify.base.config import load_config
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    13→
    14→
    15→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    16→class CommandRuntime:
    17→    """Explicit runtime dependencies shared by command handlers."""
    18→
    19→    config: dict[str, Any]
    20→    state: StateModel
    21→    state_path: Path | None
    22→
    23→
    24→def command_runtime(args) -> CommandRuntime:
    25→    """Return runtime context from explicit args.runtime or construct one."""
    26→    runtime = getattr(args, "runtime", None)
    27→    if isinstance(runtime, CommandRuntime):
    28→        return runtime
    29→
    30→    config = load_config()
    31→    state_file = state_path(args)
    32→    if isinstance(state_file, str):
    33→        state_file = Path(state_file)
    34→
    35→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    36→
    37→    return CommandRuntime(config=config, state=state, state_path=state_file)
    38→
    39→
    40→__all__ = ["CommandRuntime", "command_runtime"]
    41→

    13→COLORS = {
    14→    "reset": "\033[0m",
    15→    "bold": "\033[1m",
    16→    "dim": "\033[2m",
    17→    "red": "\033[31m",
    18→    "green": "\033[32m",
    19→    "yellow": "\033[33m",
    20→    "blue": "\033[34m",
    21→    "cyan": "\033[36m",
    22→}
    23→
    24→NO_COLOR = os.environ.get("NO_COLOR") is not None
    25→
    26→
    27→def colorize(text: str, color: str) -> str:
    28→    if NO_COLOR or not sys.stdout.isatty():
    29→        return str(text)
    30→    return f"{COLORS.get(color, '')}{text}{COLORS['reset']}"
    31→
    32→
    33→def log(msg: str) -> None:
    34→    """Print a dim status message to stderr."""
    35→    print(colorize(msg, "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    36→
    37→
    38→def print_table(
    39→    headers: list[str], rows: list[list[str]], widths: list[int] | None = None
    40→) -> None:
    41→    if not rows:
    42→        return
    43→    if not widths:
    44→        widths = [
    45→            max(len(str(h)), *(len(str(r[i])) for r in rows))
    46→            for i, h in enumerate(headers)
    47→        ]
    48→    header_line = "  ".join(h.ljust(w) for h, w in zip(headers, widths, strict=False))
    49→    print(colorize(header_line, "bold"))
    50→    print(colorize("─" * (sum(widths) + 2 * (len(widths) - 1)), "dim"))
    51→    for row in rows:
    52→        print("  ".join(str(v).ljust(w) for v, w in zip(row, widths, strict=False)))
    53→
    54→
    55→def display_entries(
    56→    args: object,
    57→    entries: Sequence[Any],
    58→    *,
    59→    label: str,
    60→    empty_msg: str,
    61→    columns: Sequence[str],
    62→    widths: list[int] | None,
    63→    row_fn: Callable[[Any], list[str]],
    64→    json_payload: dict | None = None,
    65→    overflow: bool = True,
    66→) -> bool:
    67→    """Standard JSON/empty/table display for detect commands."""
    68→    if getattr(args, "json", False):
    69→        payload = json_payload or {"count": len(entries), "entries": entries}
    70→        print(json.dumps(payload, indent=2))
    71→        return True
    72→    if not entries:
    73→        print(colorize(empty_msg, "green"))
    74→        return False
    75→    print(colorize(f"\n{label}: {len(entries)}\n", "bold"))
    76→    top = getattr(args, "top", 20)
    77→    rows = [row_fn(e) for e in entries[:top]]
    78→    print_table(list(columns), rows, widths)
    79→    if overflow and len(entries) > top:
    80→        print(f"\n  ... and {len(entries) - top} more")

1→"""Runtime compatibility governance matrix and helper predicates.
     2→
     3→This module is the single source of truth for import-boundary policy:
     4→- public runtime modules (stable entry surfaces),
     5→- deprecated compatibility modules (removal candidates),
     6→- private modules (internal implementation details).
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→from collections.abc import Iterable
    12→
    13→# Public package roots intended for runtime imports across the codebase.
    14→PUBLIC_RUNTIME_ROOTS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    15→    "desloppify.app",
    16→    "desloppify.base",
    17→    "desloppify.engine",
    18→    "desloppify.intelligence",
    19→    "desloppify.languages",
    20→)
    21→
    22→# Compatibility shims removed from runtime usage; these must not be reintroduced.
    23→SOFT_DEPRECATED_MODULES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    24→    {
    25→        "desloppify.utils",
    26→        "desloppify.file_discovery",
    27→        "desloppify.base.output_api",
    28→        "desloppify.base.output_contract",
    29→    }
    30→)
    31→
    32→SOFT_DEPRECATED_SHORT_IMPORTS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    33→    {
    34→        "utils",
    35→        "file_discovery",
    36→    }
    37→)
    38→
    39→# Private internals: only code under these roots may import them directly.
    40→PRIVATE_MODULE_PREFIXES: tuple[str, ...] = ()
    41→PRIVATE_ALLOWED_IMPORTER_PREFIXES: tuple[str, ...] = ("desloppify.base",)
    42→
    43→
    44→def is_soft_deprecated_module(module: str) -> bool:
    45→    """Return True when module is a soft-deprecated compatibility surface."""
    46→    return module in SOFT_DEPRECATED_MODULES
    47→
    48→
    49→def is_private_module(module: str) -> bool:
    50→    """Return True when module path belongs to a private internal namespace."""
    51→    normalized = module.strip()
    52→    if not normalized:
    53→        return False
    54→    for prefix in PRIVATE_MODULE_PREFIXES:
    55→        if normalized == prefix or normalized.startswith(f"{prefix}."):
    56→            return True
    57→    return False
    58→
    59→
    60→def importer_can_access_private(importer_module: str) -> bool:
    61→    """Return True when importer module is allowed to reference private APIs."""
    62→    normalized = importer_module.strip()
    63→    if not normalized:
    64→        return False
    65→    for prefix in PRIVATE_ALLOWED_IMPORTER_PREFIXES:
    66→        if normalized == prefix or normalized.startswith(f"{prefix}."):
    67→            return True
    68→    return False
    69→
    70→
    71→def iter_soft_deprecated_module_paths() -> Iterable[str]:
    72→    """Yield python-module relative paths for deprecated compatibility modules."""
    73→    for module in sorted(SOFT_DEPRECATED_MODULES):
    74→        parts = module.split(".")
    75→        yield "/".join(parts) + ".py"
    76→
    77→
    78→__all__ = [
    79→    "PRIVATE_ALLOWED_IMPORTER_PREFIXES",
    80→    "PRIVATE_MODULE_PREFIXES",
    81→    "PUBLIC_RUNTIME_ROOTS",
    82→    "SOFT_DEPRECATED_MODULES",
    83→    "SOFT_DEPRECATED_SHORT_IMPORTS",
    84→    "importer_can_access_private",
    85→    "is_private_module",
    86→    "is_soft_deprecated_module",
    87→    "iter_soft_deprecated_module_paths",
    88→]

    50→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state on missing/corruption."""
    51→    state_path = path or STATE_FILE

    62→                print(

   106→            file=sys.stderr,

   111→def _coerce_integrity_target(value: object) -> float | None:
   112→    if not is_numeric(value):
   113→        return None
   114→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, float(value)))
   115→
   116→
   117→def _resolve_integrity_target(
   118→    state: StateModel,
